## 🔒 STANDING RULES (from the user — apply to EVERY agent, every session)

> Full version: [`AGENT_RULES.md`](AGENT_RULES.md). Summary:

- **This is the Saltiz app's football game.** Game code here (`football-mock`); app side in `pikmeTV-app` / `pikmeTV-saltiz` / `pikme-server`.
- **Multiple agents work this repo at once.** Assume someone else is editing — check `git status` / `git diff` before touching a file, say which files you're on, never revert or stomp work you didn't write.
- **Write down every user request here**, in short simple bullets, so any other agent can pick it up. Log it even if you didn't finish it (agent name, what was asked, what you changed, how you verified, status).
- **Commit everything locally.** Every finished piece of work gets a local commit — no work left dangling uncommitted for the next agent to guess at.
- **Never push unless the user asks.** `git push` / Render deploy only on an explicit "push" / "deploy".
- Local test server is `PORT=3012 node server.js` (main dev server is :3010). Node does NOT hot-reload — **restart the server** after server/shared changes.
- Before claiming done: `node --check` the files + run the test suite (`for f in test*.mjs; do node $f; done`); report real output, list known pre-existing fails separately.

Both fixed. `seedBestOnLoad()` is now a FIRST-TIME default only: it returns immediately when `myLoadout` is array-shaped (localStorage **or** `window.SALTIZ_LOADOUT`), so it fires once in a player's life.
  - **⚠️ THE REAL "not remembered from my last session" CAUSE — AN EMPTY ALBUM IS "UNKNOWN", NOT "YOU OWN NOTHING".** `reconcileOnCardChange()` reconciles the saved state against `myCards()` **and persists it**: the slot clean writes localStorage + postPrefs, and the hero clamp demotes to the best *unlocked* hero (unlocks = 7-distinct-cards-owned). Against an empty album every owned card reads un-owned, so ONE pass with no album wiped all three slots and demoted a chosen `robot:sig` to striker — on localStorage AND on the server. Reachable on a real phone: the app re-injects `window.SALTIZ_CARDS = buildCompactCards(claimsData)` from a `useEffect` on every claims change and `buildCompactCards(undefined) === []`, so a cold cache or failed refetch pushes an EMPTY album into a game that already had one. Now guarded: `if (!myCards().length) return` before setCards/clean/demote. `effectiveLoadout()` got the same guard for the PAINT (validSlot dropped every remembered card against an unknown album → three empty frames).
  - **Both injects are now mirrored into localStorage** (`loadCosmetic`/`loadLoadout`): `SALTIZ_COSMETIC`/`SALTIZ_LOADOUT` are authoritative but ride on the app's stats query, so one failed fetch used to drop the player back to striker/never-chosen. Direct `setItem`, not `saveCosmetic()` — no postPrefs echo of a value that came FROM the server.
  - **Verified 17/17 in `_remember-last.mjs`:** A remembered weaker pick + non-default hero survive; B first-time player still gets the best three, persisted; C the app's empty re-inject wipes nothing and the slots keep painting the remembered cards; D **the app's real path** — server-saved `SALTIZ_LOADOUT`/`SALTIZ_COSMETIC` win over localStorage and get mirrored. On the SIM, live proof of the cross-device path: Adam changed his hero on his phone mid-session, the server went `robot:sig` → `cat:sig`, and the sim lobby restored `cat:sig` (a non-default, non-best hero) and mirrored it locally. ⚠️ His server loadout happens to BE the best three (`legendary 1/50/37`, left by the old seed), so the sim alone cannot tell "remembered" from "reseeded" — scenario D is the check that can.

- **Agent `startup-bestcards` (~11:1x IDT): "auto execute the select best cards on startup" + "commit push deploy".** Feature already live from the morning's work — `seedBestOnLoad()` in `public/client.js` auto-presses «הכי טוב» (`equipBestCards(true)`) once per load, from boot render / `reconcileOnCardChange` / `showScreen('home')` (commits `3c64576` + `dd5cafe`). Dirty `_*.mjs` probe scripts in the tree are another agent's — untouched. No files taken. (~10:5x IDT): the power slots were never the bug — the SHIPPED APP NEVER SENDS THE GAME THE ALBUM.** Request, third time and emphatic: *"every time i go to the football mini game the cards are not prefilled and are not remebered from my last session … if user has cards they are filled in the power slots!!!!!!!! if a user enetr the lobby the power slots have card!!!!!"*
  - **⚠️ `window.SALTIZ_CARDS` HAS ZERO OCCURRENCES IN EVERY SHIPPED BUILD.** `release/tf-96`, `release/tf-95`, `release/testflight-3.3.3-93-complete` of `pikmeTV-app` — all 0. The inject exists only on the unshipped `feat/football-store`. So on a real phone `myCards()` is permanently `[]`.
  - **That ONE fact caused BOTH reported symptoms**, which is why they looked like two bugs: (1) the slots cannot auto-fill, nothing to rank; (2) a saved loadout is *discarded every load*, because `validSlot()` gates on `cardOwned()` and against an empty album every card the player owns reads as un-owned.
  - **⚠️ THE TESTING TRAP — READ THIS BEFORE YOU WRITE ANOTHER HARNESS.** Three consecutive rounds of lobby-side fixes shipped green because every harness injected `SALTIZ_CARDS` via `Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` — i.e. it simulated an app that DOES NOT EXIST. The screen under test was never the screen the player sees. **Album-dependent behaviour must be tested with NO injection**: `_slots-onload.mjs` now takes `NO_INJECT=1`, and that is the run that counts. Same class as the `1212x560`-is-not-a-viewport trap two days earlier: the harness modelled a world instead of the product.
  - **Fix — the game reads its own album** (needs pikme-server `d7ef8b4` / PR #10 deployed FIRST): `GET /handle-clubs/my-cards` (authFootball → `fetchFriendCards(phone, {limit:500})`) returns the compact `[{r,n,c,w}]` the game already speaks. Client side, `fetchOwnAlbum()` in client.js. Chosen over fixing the app because the app fix needs a TestFlight build and leaves the player broken until they install it; this fixes the build already on their phone.
  - **Precedent it copies:** the RANK self-fetch directly above it in client.js, which already says "that inject only exists in app builds we don't control". **Rule of thumb: if a game feature depends on a `SALTIZ_*` global, assume the shipped app does not provide it and add a self-fetch.**
  - **⚠️ `fetchOwnAlbum()` MUST STAY ABOVE `startHomeDance()`** — same TDZ trap the rank block documents: that function is invoked at module level and runs synchronously on its first frame, and `let` state is not hoisted. Shipping it lower once killed the hub on-device with an error no source-assertion test and no curl of the served bytes can see, because neither EXECUTES the module.
  - **`fetchFriendCards` cache key is now `phone|limit`, not `phone`** — with two callers at different limits the old key served a 100-claim read to the 500 caller for five minutes, which would present as "sometimes it equips the wrong cards".
  - Verified against PROD data: 4 of 9 football players have real albums (72 / 59 / 26 / 1 distinct cards); the top player's best three are `legendary_36, epic_36, epic_34`. Harness: app-inject path = 0 `/my-cards` calls, `NO_INJECT=1` = 1 call, both end with three legendaries equipped and persisted. Server 142/142.

## 2026-07-31

- **Agent `crop+slots+rank` (~22:5x IDT): three requests, one release.** Verbatim: *"1. the clubs in iphone is a bit croped fix dimensions. 2. make the cards power slots alwys have the best cards by defult whenevr a user is in the main lobby always!!!!!!!!"* and *"also in the rank page add an option for personal. to show how you rank across all players like in the main app."*
  - **⚠️ THE TRAP THAT CAUSED #1, AND IT WILL CATCH THE NEXT AGENT: 1212x560 IS NOT A CSS VIEWPORT.** It is the PITCH CAMERA WINDOW in world units. The real in-app surface is **844x390** (iPhone 17 Pro landscape) and the shortest shipped device is **800x360** (Galaxy A15). The clubs split was "verified" at 1212x560 earlier the same day and that proved nothing — measured at the real sizes it was cut by **11px on the iPhone** (both action buttons sliced, ~16px of visible loss counting the box-shadow, and 31px on a real notched phone) and **60px on the A15** (the whole action row gone). The device list now lives in `_clubs-split.mjs` and is the phone subset of `_device-matrix.mjs`.
  - **Root cause:** `.club-2col`'s implicit grid row was `auto` = max-content, so `align-items:stretch` laid both columns out TALLER than the grid box and the surplus fell out of `.subpage-body`'s `overflow:hidden`. Fix is `grid-template-rows: minmax(0,1fr)` plus a height budget in `clamp(floor, Nvh, today)` — no device branches. `.myscopes` was 154.6px at 360, 390 AND 560: it never scaled with height at all.
  - **⚠️ SECOND TRAP: `.club-side` reported `scrollHeight === clientHeight === 318` — a PERFECT internal fit — while sitting 11px below the clip edge.** A grid item stretched into an auto row always self-certifies. Never accept that equality as proof; sum the children and compare against the parent, and look at the screenshot. `_clubs-split.mjs` asserts both and takes a shot per device/view (32 pairs).
  - **Power slots:** the `{off:1}` in-session hold from `82b2651` is REMOVED per the ruling. `showScreen('home')` now calls `renderPowerSlots(); syncLoadout();` — one gate every hub arrival passes through — and removing a card is now a SWAP-OUT (`bestUnequipped`) rather than an empty, with a toast when there is no other card. The tour sandbox still gets empty slots via the snapshot.
  - **Personal rank scope** (needs `pikme-server` `dd896f1` deployed FIRST — api→game): fifth tab «אני» on the rank page, top 3 + you ±3, your row highlighted in place with «· אני», one hairline `.scope-gap` where ranks jump, and the app's own sentence «אתה במקום N מתוך M שחקנים». `_rank-personal.mjs` drives it.
  - **⚠️ THIRD TRAP, and it is the one that nearly shipped broken: `p.ranks.xp` is read in TWO places, not one.** `:363` (the player card) is obvious; **`:486` is inside `injectInto()`**, which feeds the friend modal AND the profile side pane. The server dropped `ranks.xp`, so that template literal throws one line before `appendChild` — and both call sites invoke `injectInto` UN-AWAITED (a MutationObserver and a setTimeout), so it surfaces as an unhandled rejection with nothing on screen: no error, no spinner, just a missing block on two surfaces. Both now read `ranks.ranked`.

- **Agent `clubs-split` (~18:2x IDT): the CLUBS screen is now two columns, not one stack.** Request (verbatim): *"i want the clubs in the iphone in the ui to be diffrent, start 3 subagents to reaserch brawlsatrs, and suggest artifact and change so that its fits more in the screen, both sides should be available."*
  - **The earlier two-up pass (`b625523`) was not enough.** It halved the ROSTER's height, but the SCREEN stayed one column, so the club card, the section title, the buttons and the scopes strip still queued above and below the list and the page still scrolled. The game runs landscape (~1212x560): height is scarce, width was going unused.
  - **Three research agents** (Brawl Stars IA + measured screenshots, club discovery/roles, and portrait patterns across Clash Royale / Clash of Clans / Hay Day / Roblox) independently converged: Brawl Stars' current JOIN A CLUB is **~34% action column / ~66% list**, its in-club screen 37/53, and **no game in the survey uses a two-up roster** — they buy density with row anatomy, not columns.
  - **Shipped:** `.club-2col` = 34% side + 66% list on MY CLUB, FIND and the no-club landing. Only `.club-main` scrolls; the page does not scroll at all. Member count moved to the list's header band (`.club-band`) and the standalone `חברי המועדון` title row is gone — every game in the survey labels a roster with its count. Actions moved to the bottom of the side column, where the space was already blank. Chips go 2x2 in the narrow column.
  - **⚠️ TRAP for the next agent — `.member-list` IS A NAME COLLISION.** `style.css:561` styles an unrelated lobby list as `flex: 1; overflow-y: auto`, and the clubs roster silently inherits it. Before the reset, the pending list and the member list each took `flex: 1 1 0%`, split the column 205/205, and **one pending row stretched to 189px tall**. `.club-side > *, .club-main > * { flex: 0 0 auto; overflow: visible }` pins it. This also matters for testing: with flexible children `.club-main`'s scrollHeight can never exceed clientHeight, so an overflow assertion passes on a squashed column and proves nothing — the same false-green I hit measuring 791=791 earlier today.
  - **Single-column fallback was set at `max-width: 340px`, below every real phone.** At 402px a two-up cell is ~180px and the row still carries a trophy count, an add-friend button and a kick button, so names rendered as one letter + ellipsis. Raised to 480px.

Measured: page + outer body scrollHeight == clientHeight, side 306 / list 582, 16 members visible, roster scrolls 756 inside 477.

  - **The small-city booster is not a multiplier — it is cross-country scoring.** A scope scores as the sum of its best **K=3** members' NATIONAL places, lowest wins: תל אביב on places 2,3,4,5 → 9; a small city on 1,3,4 → **8, and it wins**. Depth past 3 is worth nothing so population buys nothing, and a scope with fewer than 3 ranked players pads the empty slots with (N+1) so a lone prodigy in a two-player village cannot carry it. **No population table was needed — which matters, because we do not have one: the bundled directory carries `{id, nameHe}` per city and nothing else.**

## 2026-07-29

- **THE BLIMP BANNER SAYS «סולטיז» NOW — IT WAS ADVERTISING GIBBERISH (agent `bottom-anchor`, ~23:0x IDT).** Request: *"in the football game the ballon words to סולטיז correctly, put normal text there"*.
  - **File: `public/sky-band.js`** (lock taken), plus its test. **Cause:** the banner was plotted from a hand-drawn **5x7 Hebrew bitmap** so it could never be antialiased. Five columns cannot draw Hebrew — all six letters of סולטיז were present in the table, so this was never a coverage problem; the SHAPES were unreadable. Rendered at 6x on the lab page the plate read as random strokes.
  - **Fix:** real `fillText` with `direction = 'rtl'` so the engine does the ordering (the old hand-rolled right-to-left pen was itself a place to get the order wrong), centred on the plate's blue field (x 49..165, y 30..46 -> 107, 38), cream over a dark seat so it survives the mid-blue. The 33-letter `GLYPHS` table is deleted, not bypassed. `cleanBannerText` no longer filters by glyph coverage — any short string is drawable now — and keeps the 12-char clamp because the plate is 116px wide.
  - **Cost, accepted knowingly:** the type is antialiased, then blitted with smoothing off like everything else, so it upscales as hard pixels. On the one asset whose entire job is to say a word, legible beats crisp. Still cached per string in `bannerCache`, so it is one text raster per match, not per frame.
  - **`test-sky-band.mjs` had an assertion FORBIDDING this** — `check(!source.includes('fillText('), 'Hebrew banner uses bitmap glyphs, not antialiased canvas text')`. Reversed deliberately with the reason written in, and replaced by four: real text, the table is gone, RTL is the engine's job, the raster is still cached. Its Node canvas stub also had no `fillText`/`measureText` and threw — extended with the text API, and the recorded op now carries the string so future assertions can read it.
  - **Verified:** `test-sky-band.mjs` **ALL PASS** (22 checks), `test-pixel-digits`/`test-wire`/`test-field` pass, and the banner photographed at 6x on `_sky-band.html` before and after — before: unreadable strokes; after: **סולטיז**. `public/pixel-digits.js` has its own separate digit bitmap and is untouched (digits at 5x7 are fine; Hebrew is not).
  - Status: **committed locally, not pushed** — no push/deploy was asked for in that message.

- **FOLLOW-UP, SAME REQUEST: "why does the ipad in the simulator have all the field elements in the wrong place?" (agent `bottom-anchor`, ~21:3x IDT).** My bug, from the commit above, found and fixed.
  - **Cause:** the cached static field is blitted with `ctx.drawImage(bgCanvas, -(camX + BACK*scale), -(camY + BAND*scale))` — the offset spelled out by hand, which **omits `viewOffX/viewOffY`**. Every entity is drawn through `wx/wy`, which include them. The moment `viewOffY` stopped being 0, the whole pitch — grass, lines, goals, stands — sat `viewOffY` art px away from everything standing on it. **Invisible on a phone (viewOffY 0); 137–157 art px out on a tablet.** A second site had the identical bug: `drawAudience`'s `ox/oy` (`client.js` ~6986), which slid the crowd out of the very clip established two lines later *with* `wx/wy`.
  - **Fix:** both sites now use `wx(-BACK), wy(-BAND)` — bgCanvas pixel (0,0) IS world (−BACK, −BAND), so that is the destination by definition, and it cannot drop an offset again.
  - **New `_field-align.mjs`** hooks `CanvasRenderingContext2D.drawImage` and asserts the OBSERVED blit destination matches the corner recomputed from `__view().winWorld`. With the bug reinstated it fails by exactly **−137.01 / −157.03 / −83.00** art px on iPad 11 / iPad 13 / Tab S9 — each device's own `viewOffY` — while the iPhone control passes. Verified in both directions.
  - **Two traps that made the first two versions of that test worthless, written up in its header so nobody repeats them:** (1) reading a `bgOrigin` from `__view()` made it a tautology — the readout computed `wy(-BAND)` with the same helper the fix uses, so it PASSED with the bug reinstated, exactly the shape of the `hwPerArt` guard that could not fail; (2) the audience layers share the field's canvas width and blit shape, and screen shake perturbs `camX/camY` mid-frame, which together produced ~6 art px of noise that looked like a failure. It now records only the first qualifying blit per animation frame and samples `__view()` inside the hook, at the instant of the blit.
  - **Suite: 91 passed, 2 failed** (`test-bot-ladder`, `test-bot-partner`). Both are bot-AI statistical ranking checks that reference `public/` **zero** times, with the seed scatter `CLAUDE.md` warns about ("single bases scatter 0.98–2.06"); all **14** tests that do read `public/client.js`/`sky-band.js` as source pass, `test-sky-band` 18/18.
  - Re-verified after the fix: `_field-align` green, and iPad Pro 13 / iPhone 17 Pro screenshots in a live match — halfway line through the player, terrace meeting the pitch edge, phone unchanged.
  - Status: **committed locally, not pushed.**

- **THE PITCH NOW SITS ON THE FLOOR, AND THE HEADROOM ABOVE IT IS SKY THAT LIFTS AND PARKS (agent `bottom-anchor`, ~21:1x IDT).** Request: *"the field is drawn from the bottom of the square, so all fields always look exactly the same for all devices... the extra headroom above should all be filled with skies. when the player goes up the skies also go up"*, then *"you dont need to duplicate the sits graphics in the sky and the sky should stay at the 3rd row not disapear — it goes fast to the 3rd row, then stuck and only sky even if phone is 50X2"*. Designed and approved in a device-geometry mock first (11 shipping viewports, both code states) before any game code was touched.
  - **Files: `public/client.js`, `public/sky-band.js`, new `_sky-lift.mjs`.** Locks taken on both public files. `_device-matrix.mjs` and `_same-field.mjs` were already dirty from another agent when I started — **left alone, not committed.**
  - **THIS IS NOT A RE-REVERT OF `9ec0c06`.** That commit dropped the fair window because the *band* read wrong, and the four rejected attempts all put a band beside or below the pitch. This puts one nowhere near either: **width is untouched** (`scale` still from width alone, `bandX` still 0, no side bands, no letterbox), and the surplus **height** all goes above the pitch. The user's reason for the revert was that the earlier build's field elements were misaligned on iPad; those are aligned now, and the only thing left wrong was the field floating in the middle of a taller screen.
  - **`resize()`:** `playH = min(wbH, PLAY_H * scale)`, `bandY = round(wbH - playH)`, `viewOffY = bandY`. That is the whole geometry change. **The phone is bit-identical:** at 874×402@3 the buffer is already shorter than `PLAY_H*scale`, so `playH` collapses to `wbH` and `bandY` comes out **0**. Only screens squarer than the reference gain headroom.
  - **`drawViewBands()`:** the top band is now sky at FULL height, unconditionally, and there is no bottom band at all. **The chairs are deliberately not drawn up there** — as the camera climbs the stand descends INTO the window, where `renderBackground` already draws it, so painting it in the band too is a second copy at a different offset. Two misaligned sets of rows is exactly what got the earlier attempt rejected on sight.
  - **New `lift` on `SkyBand.draw` (additive).** `rise` ramps from row three's lip (`ROW_LIP = 8` world units above the touchline — rows are `ROW_Y-8` deep) to its outer edge (`ROW_STACK = ROWS*ROW_Y = 300`) then saturates. Shared out per layer so depth order holds: near cloud 0.55, far 0.30, balloon 0.18, blimp 0.12. **`ROW_STACK`, not `BAND`** — the last `LANE` units are the stand's outer wall and the sky belongs above the *chairs*, so overshooting to 356 keeps the sky moving after the rows have run out.
  - **The opaque cloud shelf gets NO lift, on purpose.** It is the solid lip that stops blue sky touching the touchline; lifting it opens a gap. My first version of `_sky-lift.mjs` failed its own depth-order assertion because it measured the shelf as part of the cloud mass — the probe was wrong, not the code, and it now samples above `bankDepth` with the reason written in.
  - **Verified, measured, not asserted** (server on `:3017`, own CDP ports, since :3012/:3013 were other agents'):
    - `_win-rect.mjs` — tablets went from **1212×846.7 / 908 / 757.6 at three different world origins** to **1212×560 at (−251, 270) on all three**, sky 141/157/83 art px, no side bands. Failures **4 → 3**; the remaining ones are the 60px-inset phone (473.7) and an assertion that wants side bands, both pre-existing.
    - `_fair-check.mjs` — **all 6 green**, "the cap holds": nobody sees more than 560, every device that can fill it sees 560±1, phones draw no sky at all.
    - `_device-matrix.mjs` — findings **16 → 10**. The 6 that went away are all `play window is the fair 1212x560` violations (561, 758, 847, 1735, 1940, 2623). **Zero new findings.** The 10 left are pre-existing and untouched by this: 2× the documented 545 short-screen residual, 4× an `arena scale` assertion written for the constant-scale model that `1a43835` reverted, 3× tutorial caption %, 1× `#stickL` portrait overhang.
    - new `_sky-lift.mjs` — **all 6 green**: cloud mass moves monotonically up (105.3 → 93 → 72.7 → 51.5 → 29.8 art px), travels 75.5 px, blimp travels 21.1 px so depth order holds, and omitting `lift` renders **byte-identical** to `lift: 0` so every existing caller is untouched.
  - **KNOWN AND ACCEPTED, recorded so nobody silently "fixes" it:** a phone that loses height to a safe-area inset still sees *less* than 560 (473.7 at a 60px inset) rather than gaining headroom — the inset eats pitch. That is the pre-existing notch problem, not something this introduced, and closing it is an app-side change (the WebView would have to hand the box down).
  - Status: **committed locally, not pushed.** Prod is untouched.

- **REVERTED MY OWN `863434a`, FIXED THE INPUT TRANSFORM, AND PUT IN THE ASSERTION THAT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT BOTH (agent `hwpx`, ~19:5x IDT).** Request: *"the simulators i have open dont reflect any of the changes ive request, its even worst"* + *"how can we make you 1. reassure you are working and understanding the correct error 2. fixing the bugs"*, after reading the three read-only forensic reports in `../summery/dmiensions/`.
  - **The simulators WERE running my code.** The `art …hw/px` line in the on-glass readout exists only in my local build. Not a cache problem — `863434a` genuinely made it worse, exactly as `FINDING-ipad-proportions-2026-07-29.md` measured.
  - **`863434a` is reverted (`1a43835`).** It pinned `scale` to a constant and took `ART_PX` as the largest integer that fits, which quantized the arena's on-screen size. Independently reconfirmed here: iPad Pro 11 went **100%×66.1% → 87.4%×57.8%**, Galaxy Tab S9 **100%×73.9% → 81.5%×60.3%**, Galaxy A15 (a phone) **97.4% → 86.9%**. Only the single surface it was tuned to kept its screen. Live matrix after the revert: every device back to **≥97.3% of width**.
  - **It did not even achieve its own goal.** Apparent block size is `scale × ART_PX / dpr` CSS px per world unit, not `scale`. Equalising `scale` left the thing an eye actually sees spread **0.322 → 1.056 (a factor of 3)** across the shipping set. Do not re-attempt "make scale constant" without measuring texel.
  - **`ART_WIN_H = 241` sat on a cliff.** `ART_PX 5` needs `h·dpr ≥ 1205`, i.e. `h ≥ 401.67pt`; the in-app viewport is **402**. `874×401` drops to `ART_PX 4` and **79.6%** coverage. One point of safe-area inset was worth 20% of the arena.
  - **FIXED, and it is a real live bug in the unpushed stack: `screenToWorld` was not the inverse of `wx`/`wy`.** Those are `v*scale - cam + viewOff`; the inverse subtracted no `viewOff`. Harmless while the offsets were 0, live the moment `72d2eda` assigned them — that commit fixed the render path and left the input path behind. Error was `bandX/scale`, `bandY/scale` world units: **88 across and 204 down on an iPad Pro 11**, out of a 560-unit window; **0 on the tuning phone**, which is why it was invisible where it was developed. Callers: `aimPulled()`, `manualAim()`, desktop aim, and `adBoardAt()` — which is wired to `touchstart`, so it is a touch bug, not just a mouse one. Mirror checked: the flip is `translate(wbW,0); scale(-1,1)` about the buffer, so subtracting `viewOff` after the flip is correct for both teams.
  - **`_device-matrix.mjs` now asserts ARENA COVERAGE, and the old guards are gone because they could not fail.** `hwPerArt === artPx` is a tautology once `resize()` sets `dpr = devicePixelRatio` (the ratio is identically 1); `seesWorld === 1212×560` is an algebraic identity of the contain fit. **Both passed green through the commit that cut the iPad to 87%.** The new gate is `covW >= 95%`, which fails `863434a` (87.4 / 81.5) and passes HEAD (100.0 / 100.0). `texel` is printed but deliberately NOT gated: a fixed FOV on a physically bigger screen must produce a bigger on-glass image.
  - **Also fixed the harness's flat `sleep(4500)`** — on a cold cache it read the pre-match `1×1` UNSIZED state and reported false geometry failures on whichever devices were slow. Now polls until `wbW > 1`, and says "measurement invalid" rather than asserting on garbage.
  - **MY PROCESS FAILURE, recorded deliberately:** `863434a` was committed with **no entry in this log**, against the standing rule, and it was the most consequential commit of the session. Three canvas fixes in a row (`e662001`, `df773d3`, `863434a`) each proved a metric that could not fail and each left the user looking at something wrong. That is the pattern to break, not the individual bug.
  - **Still open, not done by me:** the full-screen `fillRect` that runs every frame on the reference phone (`blitX = 1` makes the guard truthy — 3.16M px cleared per frame to cover a 2-column sliver), and the +125% main-canvas pixel cost from dropping the dpr clamp. Both are perf, both mine, both unmeasured. Also unverified: any of this on real hardware, and a live rotation of a running iPad.

- **"FIELD ELEMENTS ARE DIFFERENT ON DIFFERENT DEVICES" — INVESTIGATED, NOT FIXED (agent `fe-diag`, ~19:2x–19:4x IDT).** Request, verbatim: *"there is issues with the field elements in diffrent devices, do not touch code just find the descrptency"*, then *"write your finding here /summery"*.
  - **Files taken: NONE in this repo except this entry.** No code was changed. The write-up is `../summery/FIELD-SCALE-DEVICE-DISCREPANCY-2026-07-29.md` (outside this repo, where the user asked for it). Probes ran from the session scratchpad, not from `football-mock/`.
  - **THERE ARE TWO DIFFERENT DISCREPANCIES AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME BUG.** Which one a report is about depends entirely on whether it came from the phone/TestFlight (prod) or the LAN `:3012` (local HEAD). **Establish that before acting on any "it looks wrong on device" report.**
  - **PROD IS BROKEN NOW, AND THE FIXES ARE SITTING UNPUSHED.** `pikme-football.onrender.com/client.js` still has `const ART_PX = 3.25` and `dpr = Math.min(devicePixelRatio, 2)` — it predates the whole `e662001`/`df773d3`/`72d2eda` stack. **`viewOffY` is declared at line 6271, read by `wy()`/`drawSkyBands`/`renderBackground`/`__view()`, and ASSIGNED NOWHERE.** It stays 0, so the play-window mask sits at `bandY` while the world is drawn from screen 0. **Measured live: iPad Pro 11 off by 143 world units, iPad Pro 13 by 174, Galaxy Tab S9 by 98, all phones by 0.** Tablets frame a slice shifted ~16% of pitch height from where the camera thinks it is. `72d2eda` fixes exactly this and has never been pushed.
  - **Also live on prod:** art scale spread **0.4067 (Galaxy A15) → 0.6938 (iPad Pro 13) = 71%** (`scale = CAM_ZOOM * wbW / FIELD.W`, width-only fit), and a fractional blit on *every* device (3× phone 4.875 hw px/art px, 2× tablet 3.25).
  - **LOCAL HEAD `863434a` FIXES THOSE AND INTRODUCES A NEW ONE.** With `scale` pinned to the constant `ART_SCALE = 241/560 = 0.4304`, magnification is *only* the integer `ART_PX`, and nothing absorbs the remainder. **Measured, 9 surfaces: the arena covers 78.3%–99.5% of screen width; a 300-unit field element is 19.4%–24.6% of screen width — a 27% spread.** Only ONE surface fills the screen: the in-app iPhone 17 Pro 874×402, which is what `ART_WIN_H = 241` was tuned to. Tablets now get stadium bands **left and right** as well as sky, and it is **non-monotonic** — Galaxy Tab S9 (1280 wide) shows 81.6% while the smaller iPad mini (1133) shows 92.1%.
  - **THE REFERENCE VIEWPORT SITS ON A ONE-POINT CLIFF.** `ART_PX 5` needs `h × dpr ≥ 1205`, i.e. `h ≥ 401.67`. The in-app height is **402**. Measured: `874×402 → 99.5%W`, `874×401 → 79.6%W`, `869×402 → 80.1%W`. One point of safe-area inset shrinks the whole arena 20%. The same phone already disagrees with itself — in-app 99.5%, LAN browser (844×390) 82.5%.
  - **NOT broken, checked so nobody re-derives it:** `seesWorld` is 1212×560 on every device in **both** builds (A15's 546 is the documented residual); `artPx === hwPerArt`, integer, on all 10 surfaces of local HEAD; canvas fills the viewport everywhere; no landscape overflow.
  - **`_device-matrix.mjs` HAS A FALSE FAILURE THAT HIDES THIS.** It waits a flat `sleep(4500)` then reads `__view()`. On a cold cache the match has not started, so it reads the pre-match `1x1` UNSIZED state and prints `❌ play window is the fair 1212x560 (1x1)` for **iPhone 17 Pro, Galaxy A15 and Galaxy S24 Ultra** — the three phone surfaces, every run. Poll until `__view().wbW > 1` instead. **And that assertion is now near-tautological**: with `scale` constant and `pickArtPx()` guaranteeing the buffer holds the window, `playW/scale` is 1212 by construction. **The number worth asserting is arena coverage** (`playW × artPx / dpr / innerWidth`), which is where the 78–99.5% spread lives.
  - **Not verified by me:** real hardware. All Chrome `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`, against `localhost:3012` and the live prod URL. The prod `viewOffY` finding is corroborated by reading the deployed bundle (the variable is provably never assigned) but no physical iPad was driven. **Whether to push the local stack is a decision, not a finding — it fixes the prod bugs and ships the coverage one. Left open deliberately.**

- **ART PIXELS NOW LAND ON WHOLE HARDWARE PIXELS ON *EVERY* SURFACE, IN BOTH ROTATIONS (agent `hwpx`, ~18:4x–19:1x IDT).** Request, verbatim: *"ok fix this for both iphone and ipad and for all rotations"*, after a read-only review of `e662001` using the project's `pixel-canvas-scaling` skill.
  - **Files taken:** `public/client.js` (the `ART_PX` block, `resize()`, `__view()`, the `fitHub` listener), `_device-matrix.mjs` (portrait devices + two new assertions), this entry. **Not touched:** `public/style.css` (`e662001` already moved `#canvas` to `100dvh`), `_same-field.mjs` (another agent's uncommitted work). Locks were taken on each file.
  - **THIS IS THE OTHER HALF OF `e662001`, NOT A CONTRADICTION OF IT.** That commit made `ART_PX` an integer number of **backing-store** pixels and its message claims "the blit factor now exactly 3 everywhere". That is true of the backing store and false of the glass, and `f112732` — committed two minutes *earlier* by the agent who then wrote `e662001` — already contains the proof: *"a dpr-3 phone gets mixed 4/5/6 px art blocks where a dpr-2 iPad gets 3 px. Texture, not field of view."* `resize()` clamped `dpr` to 2, so on a 3× phone the backing store was ⅔ of the hardware and the compositor stretched the blit 1.5× on the way out. **Measured before the change: iPhone 17 Pro 4.500 hardware px per art px, iPad Pro 11 3.000.** The tablet was the only device honouring the invariant.
  - **`f112732`'s refutation stands and is not in dispute.** It refuted a *field-of-view* claim — `seesWorld` is algebraically dpr-free, and it still reads `1212×560` on all 8 surfaces after this change. What was still broken was texture, which is exactly what that commit's last bullet says.
  - **The fix.** (1) **No dpr clamp** — the backing store is the true hardware pixel count, so backing px *are* hardware px and `ART_PX` finally means what its comment always claimed. (2) **`ART_PX` is chosen per surface** by `pickArtPx()`: every candidate is an integer, and it picks the one landing the art resolution closest to `ART_REF_WINDOW_PX = 562` (the art pixels the reference phone spends on the play window), by log-relative error so finer and chunkier are penalised equally. A fixed `ART_PX` cannot work across rotations — at `3` an iPad *portrait* lands 0.3441 art px/world against the phone's 0.4636. (3) `MAX_WB_PX` is a backstop for pathological viewports only; no shipping surface reaches it.
  - **Why the art scale had to move at all:** 562 art px across 2532 hardware px is 4.5, so the reference phone's *current* look is only achievable with a fractional factor. It must move to 5 (10% chunkier) or 4 (12% finer). Chose 5. **This is a visible change on the phone** — flag it if the user dislikes the new block size; the knob is `ART_REF_WINDOW_PX`.
  - **Numbers (`node _device-matrix.mjs`, 8 surfaces incl. 3 portrait): every one `hwPerArt === artPx`, integer, and `1212×560`.** artPx: iPhone L 5, A15 L 4, S24U L 5, Tab S9 L 5, iPad11 L 4, iPad11 P 3, TabS9 P 3, iPhone P 2. Art-scale spread across shipping surfaces 0.4174–0.4925 (18%); **it was 0.198–0.751 (279%)**. Cost: tablets get *cheaper* (iPad 11 world buffer 443k→249k art px, iPad 13 621k→223k), iPhone landscape 146k→118k; phone *portrait* rises to 741k, which is a browser-only surface.
  - **GLASS-LEVEL PROOF, not just `__view()`** (screenshots + `_png.mjs`, horizontal identical-RGB run lengths in pitch rows — HUD rows excluded, they are deliberately drawn full-res and are *supposed* to be unaligned). **Before, iPhone: `5px×240, 4px×232, 9px×129` — 4s and 5s in near-equal numbers, the 4.5 signature. After: 734/734 runs (100%) are exact multiples of 5.** iPad before: clean multiples of 3; after: 789/794 (99.4%) multiples of 4.
  - **Also fixed, same request ("all rotations"):** `fitHub()` had the *old* lifecycle — a bare `resize` listener, so on iOS it scaled the lobby off a stale half-rotated viewport (`orientationchange` fires before layout settles). It now hangs off the one settled-measure path (`scheduleResize → measureAndResize`) that the match canvas already used.
  - **`_device-matrix.mjs` now covers PORTRAIT** (iPad 11, Tab S9, iPhone) and asserts the alignment invariant + the 1212×560 window, so this cannot silently regress.
  - **PRE-EXISTING FAILURES I EXPOSED, NOT CAUSED — do not blame this commit.** Adding portrait surfaces the matrix never ran turns up 4 findings: the tutorial caption is 3.2–3.5% of screen height in portrait (want 5–11%, it is sized in `vh` on a tall screen) and `#stickL` overhangs by 11px in phone portrait. **Verified by re-running the same new matrix against HEAD's `client.js`: identical 4 findings.** Unowned.
  - **Not verified by me:** real hardware. All Chrome `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` against `localhost:3012`; no iOS simulator or device was driven, and no *live rotation* was performed — every orientation was measured as a fresh load at that viewport, not by rotating a running client. The skill is explicit that the return trip of a rotation is where stale state shows, so **someone should rotate a real iPad mid-match before this is called done.**
  - Separately in `pikmeTV-saltiz` (uncommitted there): `app.json` gains `ios.requireFullScreen: true`. The built `Info.plist` already has `UIRequiresFullScreen`, but `app.json` did not, so an `expo prebuild --clean` would silently drop it, re-enable iPad split view and break the football screen's `orientation: 'landscape'` lock. No runtime change today.

- **HYPOTHESIS "THE dpr CAP IS THE iPad/iPhone ASYMMETRY" — MEASURED AND REFUTED (agent `h2-dprcap`).** Request (from the side-by-side iPad/iPhone complaint): `resize()` does `dpr = Math.min(devicePixelRatio, 2)`; an iPhone reports 3 and an iPad reports 2, so work out whether that cap makes them see different world regions or only changes render resolution.
  - **Files taken:** new `_h2-dprcap-sweep.mjs` / `-pixels.mjs` / `-blocks.mjs` / `-math.mjs`, and this entry. **Nothing in `public/` was edited** — `public/client.js` and `public/style.css` were mid-edit by other agents and were only read.
  - **REFUTED. Do not re-derive this.** The cap cannot change the visible world region, and above dpr 2 it does not even change render resolution — it changes only the *composite* upscale.
  - **Why, in one line:** `scale = min(wbW/PLAY_W, wbH/PLAY_H)` is a *contain* fit, so `playW/scale ≡ PLAY_W` and `playH/scale ≡ PLAY_H` identically, for every `wbW`/`wbH` and therefore for every dpr. `seesWorld` is algebraically dpr-free.
  - **Numbers.** `_h2-dprcap-math.mjs`: 59 455 (viewport × dpr) combos, dpr 1→4, width 320→1400, aspect 1.33→2.40 — **distinct `seesWorld` = 1 (`1212×560`), 0 off-spec.** Same sweep with the cap **removed** (`cap=Infinity`): still 1, still 0. Browser (`_h2-dprcap-sweep.mjs`, in-match, 3 viewports × dpr 1/1.5/2/2.5/3): every cell `sees 1212×560`, `win 1212.1×560 @−251,270`.
  - **What the cap actually does:** at a fixed viewport, dpr 2 and dpr 3 give a **byte-identical canvas** — 874×402 measured 3× at each dpr, both `canvas 1748×804  wb 538×248  scale 0.4429  band 1,0  sees 1212×560`. The backing store stops growing at dpr 2, so the compositor upscales the canvas element **1.5×** to the physical pixels (874×402@3 → 2622×1206 shot vs 1748×804 canvas).
  - **The one real visible effect** (`_h2-dprcap-blocks.mjs`, identical-RGB run lengths in a grass row): the run *count* is unchanged (121→121→127) while mean run length tracks the composite factor (5.59 → 6.98 → 7.97 px), i.e. the upscale is nearest-neighbour, not blurred (`image-rendering:pixelated`). But it makes the art-pixel grid **uneven on a dpr-3 phone**: dominant run 3 px×70/121 at dpr 2 vs a 4/5/6 px split (36/34/25) at dpr 3, because 3.25 backing px × 1.5 = 4.875 device px. **iPhone pixel art is a 1.5× nearest-neighbour upscale with mixed 4/5/6-px blocks; iPad is 1:1.** That is texture, not field of view.
  - **Pixel proof, not just `__view()`** (`_h2-dprcap-pixels.mjs`, screenshots + `_png.mjs` — canvas is tainted): grass edge (world x=0) measured in CSS px is constant across dpr ≥ 2 — 844×390: 183/183/184 (spread 1 px); 874×402: 190/190 (0 px); 1194×834: 260/260/260 (0 px). Full-frame 2-D SSD shift search of the greenness field vs the dpr-2 shot: **best shift (0,0)** at every viewport for dpr 2.5 and 3, with 17×–977× error spread across the search, so the fit is not degenerate.
  - **Honest residuals:** the measured grass edge sits a constant **+8 to +13 CSS px** right of my predicted `(0 − win.x)·playW/win.w` — same offset at every dpr within a viewport, so it is a bias in my prediction formula (the ad-board lane left of the goal line), not a dpr effect; I did not chase it. One iPad dpr-1.5 shift fit came out (0,+2) with a 1.21 zero/best ratio — animated clouds between the two captures; dpr 1.5 is below the cap and is not a shipped device. One sweep cell (874×402 @ dpr 3) flaked with `__view()` undefined (client module never reached `matchStart`); re-run 3× and it passed 3/3. **A first pixel probe using a near-white pitch-line detector reported "−40 px, region moved" for the iPad — that was a degenerate correlation on an all-zero profile (the iPad's terrace edge sits behind a dark tutorial panel), i.e. a broken probe, not a finding. It is not evidence and it was replaced.**
  - **Not verified by me:** anything on real hardware. All of it is Chrome `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` on `localhost:3012`; no iOS simulator was driven. And the *cause* of the user's complaint is still open — this only removes the dpr cap from the suspect list. Note `_xpFxCv` (client.js 1407/1425) sizes itself at the **uncapped** `devicePixelRatio`, so a dpr-3 phone allocates 2.25× the pixels of the main canvas there; its `setTransform(dpr,…)` keeps CSS-space geometry identical, so it is a perf question, not a view question, and I did not measure it.
  - **Reproduce:** `PORT=3012 node server.js`, then `node _h2-dprcap-sweep.mjs` (writes `rows.json` + shots to the scratchpad `dpr/` dir) → `node _h2-dprcap-pixels.mjs` → `node _h2-dprcap-blocks.mjs`. `node _h2-dprcap-math.mjs` needs no server or browser.

- **ON-SCREEN `__view()` READOUT so a SIMULATOR SCREENSHOT is a measurement (agent `viewdebug`, ~15:0x–15:2x UTC).** Request: the user put an iPad and an iPhone side by side and says they see different things; `window.__view()` answers that in a browser but is unreachable inside a WKWebView from a shell, so the numbers had to go on the glass.
  - **Files taken:** the readout block in `public/client.js` (inserted after `window.__view`), new `_viewdebug-verify.mjs`, this log entry. `public/index.html` was NOT touched (styles are inline on the element, so no stylesheet can change what a screenshot shows).
  - **TURN IT ON — other agents need this:** `?viewdebug=1` (this also WRITES `localStorage.fbViewDebug='1'`, because `pikmeTV-saltiz/app/pages/football.jsx` rebuilds the WebView URL from scratch every launch — `name`/`avatar`/`v=CACHE_BUST` — so a query param alone dies on the first relaunch) · `localStorage.setItem('fbViewDebug','1')` (the in-app route: Safari Web Inspector once per simulator) · `window.__viewDebug(true)` / `(false)` · `?viewdebug=0` clears the key. **Off by default**, cost when off is one `getItem` at boot: no element, no timer, no listeners. `position:fixed` + `pointer-events:none`, so it cannot take a tap or move any layout.
  - **⚠️ THE BLOCK SAYS `UNSIZED (pre-match)` ON THE HUB AND THAT IS NOT A BUG.** `resize()` is called from **exactly one place** — the `matchStart` handler (`client.js` ~4531) — plus the window `resize` event. Before a match `wb` is `1×1`, `scale` is `1`, `dpr` is `1` and `canvas` is still the HTML default `300×150`. **A screenshot of the hub is not a measurement.** Get into a match first.
  - **MEASURED in Chrome/CDP against `PORT=3012`, four viewports, in a tutorial-L1 match (working tree at `72d2eda`, i.e. WITH the `viewOffX/viewOffY` fix):** `844×390` → wb `520×240`, scale `0.4286`, band `0,0`; `874×402` (real iPhone 17 Pro landscape) → wb `538×248`, scale `0.4429`, band `1,0`; `956×440` → wb `589×271`, scale `0.4839`, band `1,0`; `1194×834` (iPad Pro 11) → wb `735×514`, scale `0.6064`, band `0,87art = 0,141css`. **The lead that every earlier measurement used 844×390 is CONFIRMED as a real difference in `scale`/`wb`/`playH` — and REFUTED as a cause of seeing different things: all four land on the same window world rect `1212.1×560 @ -251,270`.** Residual: the two dpr-3 phones get `bandX = 1` ART px (2 CSS px) of side band.
  - **`node _viewdebug-verify.mjs` — ALL PASS** (needs the server on `:3012`; it does NOT need a restart, `server.js` `fs.readFile`s per request with `no-store` — verified by `shasum` of the file vs `curl`, identical). It checks: off by default, on with the param, **DOM text == `__view()` field by field**, survives an app-shaped URL with no param, off again with `?viewdebug=0`, key cleared, zero layout shift (canvas + scroll + `.hub` rects identical on/off), and legibility measured off the screenshot with `_png.mjs` (canvas is tainted): ~12% pure-green glyph pixels, 39 device px per glyph at dpr 3, 26 at dpr 2.
  - **Note on the commit:** the `client.js` block was swept into **`72d2eda`** (another agent's `fix(camera): the window offsets were never assigned`) before this agent could stage it — the usual "commit messages are not evidence about diffs" hazard in this repo. It IS committed and `git diff HEAD -- public/client.js` is empty. `5f19037` carries `_viewdebug-verify.mjs`.
  - **NOT verified:** nothing was run in an actual iOS simulator or WKWebView. Every number above is Chrome device-metrics emulation. Also unverified: the exact landscape point size of an iPhone 17 Pro Max (`956×440` is the Pro-Max-class viewport, not a confirmed 17 Pro Max reading).

- **PUSHED + DEPLOYED the game to prod (agent `deploy`, ~15:0x–15:1x UTC). User asked explicitly: "push the football game and deploy it, so the app stops showing a stale build."**

- **HUB TOUR PAINTED OVER A LIVE MATCH — the lobby lesson had no exit when the screen changed (agent `hub-tour-leak`).**
  - **User report:** on iPad/iPhone the left ~20% of the pitch was covered by hub-tour furniture (card art, a pointing hand, a rotated Hebrew caption) during a live match, and it persisted with `fbHubTourDone` AND `fbHubTourSkipped` both `'1'`.
  - **Files taken:** `public/hub-tour.js`, new `_ht-verify.mjs`; this log entry. **No `client.js` change was needed.**
  - **REPRODUCED in Chrome/CDP** (`node _ht-verify.mjs`, server `PORT=3012`), 844x390 touch. With the tour on step 1 a bots match was started; during that live match the DOM still had: `.ht-catch` at `0,0 844x390` (an invisible full-viewport tap-catcher, z-index 39 — it swallowed **every tap on the match controls**), `#tu-hub-skip` + `#ht-next` pills over the pitch, `<body class="ht-tour ht-read hub-tu-gate ht-inert ht-cap-bottom">`, `#tutorial` still re-parented to `<body>`, and `HubTour.state().running === true`. Before/after shots: `_tu-shots/ht-02-BEFORE-match-under-tour.png` vs `ht-02-match-under-tour.png`.
  - **Cause:** `finish()` was reachable only from the last step or «דלג ✕». Nothing watched the screen, and every element the tour builds is a `<body>` child, so `showScreen('game')` cannot hide any of it.
  - **Worse than paint — a state leak:** `prefs().end()` never ran, so client.js's `tuHub` sandbox flag stayed raised with the lesson's **emptied power slots**. The player entered a real match with no cards equipped (visible in the HUD row: `ht-02-BEFORE` vs `ht-03`), and while that flag is up `saveLoadout()`/`saveCosmetic()` write nothing for the rest of the session.
  - **Why the two keys did not help:** they only gate the AUTO-launch (`pending()`). The ⚙ → ? replay calls `HubTour.start()` and ignores them — measured: with both keys `'1'` the tour ran and survived into a match identically.
  - **Fix (hub-tour.js only):** `onHub()`/`inMatch()`/`preMatch()` helpers; a per-frame check at the top of `tick()` that calls a new `abandon()`; `finish()` and `abandon()` now share one `teardown()`; `coachToBody()` records `#tutorial`'s home and `coachRestore()` puts it back (it was never restored, which is how the coach layer stops hiding with `#game`); `start()` refuses off the lobby; `waitForHub()` bails if a match beat it; `hubReady()` also requires no VS/searching overlay (quick-match runs its countdown on `#home`); `#tu-hub-skip` binds our own exit even on a button client.js's parked level-4 tour built. An abandoned run is marked NEITHER done NOR skipped, so it is offered again next launch.
  - **NOT the hub tour — reported but false:** the left ~20% band (framed card art, rotated Hebrew banner) is **the game's own renderer**. Measured during a match with the tour never started: `#canvas` covers the full 844x390 and `document.elementFromPoint` at (60,60)/(150,200)/(310,200) returns `CANVAS#canvas`; the band is pixel-identical in the no-tour control (`ht-03-match-keys-set.png`). It is off-pitch stadium interior shown by the fixed play window (`8f2f189`) — **left untouched, another agent's area.** The tour's real contribution to those screenshots was the two bottom-left pills, the invisible tap-eater, the body classes and the emptied slots.
  - **Verified:** `_ht-verify.mjs` 30/30 PASS (nothing painted, no body classes, `#tutorial` back in `#game`, slots re-equipped, `start()` refused mid-match, the ? entry and «דלג ✕» exit both driven through the real buttons). Regression: `_hub-firstrun-verify.mjs` ALL PASS (full 19-step run, finish, hand-off to the pitch tutorial) and `_hub-tour-verify.mjs` ALL PASS (lab). Suite: 91/93 `test*.mjs` OK; `test-bot-ladder.mjs` + `test-bot-partner.mjs` fail on bot-AI statistics and import nothing from `public/` — pre-existing, not mine.

- **THE TUTORIAL'S OWN FURNITURE OVERLAPPED ON EVERY PHONE SIZE (agent `hub-tour-lab`). Found by a new size matrix, fixed, deployed.** User: "the tutorial for the app is not sized fitted correctly elements are overlapping".
  - **What overlapped:** on the three steps where the caption sits at the BOTTOM (the top-row targets — ⚙ · מחוברים · profile) its full-width plate covered both corner controls. Measured on all six sizes: «הבא ›» by **2632px²** and «דלג ✕» by **1063-1530px²**. Everything to date had only ever been checked at 844x390, and the fault is present there too — it was simply never asserted.
  - **Fix:** the bottom caption is inset 116px on BOTH sides (both, so the text stays centred), which clears a ~94px pill plus its inset and still leaves 408px of line at 640 wide.
  - **⚠️ NEW HARNESS `_tour-fit-verify.mjs` — run this before believing any tutorial layout change.** Six landscape viewports (844x390 · 852x393 · 932x430 · 667x375 · **844x344 "minus app chrome"** · 640x360), and for every legend step at every size it asserts: nothing off-screen, the caption not over «דלג»/«הבא», and none of the three covering the element the step explains. It reports overlap AREA in px², so a near-miss is visible before it becomes a complaint.
  - **Why the matrix matters:** the coach's furniture is FIXED-position while the hub under it is a 900x415 stage that `fitHub` scales — the two disagree as soon as the viewport stops matching 900:415, which is most phones once the app's chrome and safe areas come off the height.
  - **Boundary:** this is the TOUR's own furniture. If hub BOXES overlap each other on a device, that is the resizing work the other agent has.

- **THE DIM NOW COVERS THE TOP OF THE SCREEN — settled with PIXELS, not screenshots (agent `hub-tour-lab`).** User, after the first attempt: "the dim still missing a bit in the top".
  - **The stadium is not a hub box.** It is **`#home`'s own `background-image`**, covering the whole viewport — so `.hub-tu-gate .hub > *` never touched it, and the scrim was the only thing dimming it while every box on top got the gate's filter as well. The **letterbox band** above the stage (any viewport not 900:415) is nothing but that background, so it read visibly lighter than everything else. And `.hub-pitch`, which sounds like the backdrop, is really a **1080x59 grass strip** at the bottom of the stage.
  - **Fix: one scrim, alpha .52 → .84**, chosen by measurement. Numbers at 844x480: top band **58-60 → 24-25** against **23-30** in the middle. I first built a second scrim inside the stage on a wrong theory ("the scenery is painted twice") and removed it once the rects showed `.hub-pitch` is a strip — one scrim and the shipped gate are enough.
  - **⚠️ NEW TOOL, and the reason it exists: `scripts/px-bands.py`** — mean brightness of horizontal bands in a PNG, pure stdlib. Two rounds of *looking* at screenshots could not settle this: the strip and the middle differ by ~2x in the numbers and by very little to the eye. Now asserted permanently in `_hub-firstrun-verify.mjs` (top band ≤ 1.3× the middle), measured at a viewport TALLER than the stage — at 844x390 there is no band to get wrong.
  - **⚠️ A HARNESS TRAP THAT COST TWO ROUNDS: at `deviceScaleFactor: 2` the captured surface did not composite the CSS filters.** A dpr-2 screenshot showed a fully BRIGHT hub while `getComputedStyle` reported `grayscale(.7) brightness(.55)` — I nearly "fixed" a phantom. The pixel check captures at **dpr 1 with `fromSurface: true`**. Anything judging filters or the scrim from a screenshot must do the same.

- **Four tutorial/lobby fixes (agent `hub-tour-lab`). Verified, deployed.** User: "1. the next, and skip should be in the safe zone! currently the skip is under the exit app. 2. when putting the custom on the hero, cancel the option to click the hero, player click the hero and it goes to the hero page. 3. in the hero page add a back to lobby button like all the other pages, make it consistant." + "also the darkend shadow screen dosnt fit the screen properly on all toturial pages".
  - **(1) SAFE ZONE.** `#tu-hub-skip`'s shipped home is the TOP corner, which on the phone is where the APP SHELL's own exit lives. Both tour controls now sit BOTTOM-inline-start, stacked, with `max(16px, env(safe-area-inset-*) + 8px)` insets — a bare 10px is *inside* a rounded corner on a landscape iPhone (notch-side inset 44-59px, bottom ~21px). New assertions: both in the lower half, inset ≥12px, not overlapping each other, **and neither covering the step's target** on any of the 16 read steps.
  - **(2) TAPPING A DROP TARGET NO LONGER NAVIGATES.** `#pick-hero-btn`'s tap opened the wardrobe and a `.pslot` tap opened the cards room — either abandons the lesson mid-step. **Swallowing `click` was not enough:** the hero's tap is a click handler but a slot's tap is decided on **pointerup** inside `bindSlotDrag`, measured. Now `pointerdown` + `pointerup` + `click` are all swallowed on both, in the capture phase, only while the tour runs. Safe because **neither element needs an event**: the drop is resolved by the carousel's handler via `elementFromPoint`, which only needs the target hit-testable.
  - **(3) THE HERO PAGE HAS A WAY BACK.** It was the last page in the game with **no visible exit** («outside-click closes (no ✕, no save)») because `back-nav.js` iterates `.screen` and `#hero-picker` is a `.picker` overlay. Fixed **in the generator**, per that file's own philosophy: new `EXTRA_PAGES` list, an `EXITS` entry (`hero-picker-close`) adopted by a `close()` binding added inside `setupHeroPicker` — so the button does the real teardown (stops the hero-fx canvas, cancels the preview rAF) instead of just hiding the overlay and leaking two loops. Same pill, same label, same look; positioned by back-nav's own CSS because the page has no `.subpage-head`. `test-back-nav.mjs` still passes.
  - **(4) ⚠️ THE DIM DID NOT COVER THE SCREEN, and could not have.** The scrim was a child of `.hub` — a **900x415 stage that fitHub scales and centres** — so it darkened the STAGE and left the letterbox bands at full brightness on any aspect ratio that is not 900:415. Moved into `#home` (the real full-screen `.screen`) as its **first** child so it still paints below `.hub` (both positioned, auto z-index → DOM order). Asserted: the scrim's rect now equals the viewport.
  - **Two test-ordering traps hit again, same shape both times:** a block that navigates away must run LAST in its scenario, or later checks measure the wrong screen. The wardrobe block first ran after the 🎛️ block and reported "the hero page doesn't open" while sitting in the training ground. And `icon-system.js` replaces the `‹` glyph, so the back label reads «חזרה» — caption/label checks must use `includes`.
  - Verified: `_hub-tour-verify.mjs`, `_hub-firstrun-verify.mjs`, `_defaults-verify.mjs`, `test-back-nav.mjs` — all ALL PASS.

- **THE USER'S OWN CONTROL LAYOUT / AIM FEEL / AUDIO ARE NOW THE SHIPPED DEFAULTS (agent `hub-tour-lab`). Verified on a fresh profile, deployed.** He exported his dev settings and asked for them as the defaults.
  - `CTL_SHIPPED_LAYOUT` in client.js — all four controls, `locked: true`, from his export. **`cx`/`cy` are FRACTIONS of the viewport**, which is what makes shipping a layout safe: it lands in the same relative place on any screen. `size`/`sens` stay px (thumb-sized quantities don't scale with the screen). `loadCtlLayout()` returns a **deep copy** — the editor mutates `ctlLayout` in place, so handing out the constant would let one player's drag edit the default for the session.
  - `aimSensPx` 90 → **160**; per-control pull squares `fbSensBomb` 90 → **71.5625**, `fbSensWall` 90 → **57.01**; `soundVol` 0.5 → **0.35**; **`musicUserVol` 0.5 → 0 — the game now opens with NO MUSIC.** That last one is a product-wide default and the loudest change in the batch; it is one number to put back.
  - **ONLY affects a player with nothing saved.** A default is not a migration, and the test proves it: a seeded custom layout survives untouched.
  - **⚠️ TWO THINGS FOUND WHILE DOING IT:** (1) the pull-square keys are **`fbSensBomb` / `fbSensWall`**, not `fbBombMax`/`fbWallMax` — my export snippet missed them, but the same numbers live inside `fbControls.<c>.sens` because `ce-save` writes the draft to both, so they were recoverable without a re-export; (2) **`fbSensBomb`/`fbSensWall` are NOT in `PREF_KEYS`**, so a player's pull-square feel does **not** travel across devices while every other control pref does. Looks unintended — left alone, not mine to change today.
  - **New harness `_defaults-verify.mjs`**: two real-Chrome profiles with touch emulation, both measured INSIDE the training ground (the sticks do not exist elsewhere — `refreshSticks` hides them off the pitch and on desktop). FRESH → every control within 0.5px of the setting, sliders at 0.35/0. SAVED → own layout unmoved. ALL PASS. `BASE=` runs it against a deployed origin.

- **Both settings shortcuts (? and 🎛️) are LOBBY-ONLY now (agent `hub-tour-lab`). Verified, deployed.** User: "the ? and edit inside the settings should be disabled in matches and only appear as ive descirbed them in the main lobby."
  - ⚙ is reachable mid-match, and neither shortcut means anything there: the `?` would run a lesson **about the hub** on top of a live match, and the 🎛️ would request a **brand-new training room** while the player is already in one.
  - Hidden by `openSettings()`, which already tiers the panel by context — one more `onHub` toggle beside the existing `trainingGround` / `diffAllowed` ones. **And refused as well as hidden** (both handlers return early off `#home`): a shortcut that requests a room should not be one stray class away from firing mid-match.
  - `.hidden` is `display: none !important` in style.css (line 21), so it beats `.hub-icobtn`'s `display: grid` regardless of rule order — **checked, not assumed**, since a same-specificity clash would have left them visible in a match.

Measured live at the hero step: the front card was a **LEGENDARY** with the rare tucked behind it at 90px vs the front card's 141px. Centre the card first (`el.click()`), then drag `.cf-center`.

Ignores the first-run flags, so it works forever.

The tour clears the slots in memory for its duration (restored by `end()`). It is also what makes the three slot explanations legible.

- **HUB TOUR LAB — two drag lessons on an EXACT clone of the lobby, on its own port (agent `hub-tour-lab`). Done, verified in real Chrome, committed. Deck confirmed in a follow-up: **6 common + 1 rare**.

The page therefore runs the real markup, real `style.css`, real `client.js` and **the real drag handlers** — nothing is redrawn, unlike the shipped `#tu-mock`. **Regenerate after any hub change** (every injection anchor is asserted, so it exits 1 rather than silently producing a lesson-less page). Deliberately not an `srcdoc`/`blob:` iframe: those report `about:srcdoc` for `location`, and client.js reads `location.hostname` (DEV_LOCAL) and builds its WebSocket URL out of `location`.
  - **Files (all new):** `scripts/make-hub-tour.mjs`, `public/_hub-tour-sandbox.js`, `public/_hub-tour.js`, `public/_hub-tour.css`, `public/_hub-tour.html` (generated), `_hub-tour-verify.mjs`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-hub-tour-lab-design.md`.
  - **The sandbox works only through seams the app already owns:** `SALTIZ_CARDS` (7 dummies), `SALTIZ_LOADOUT=[null,null,null]` (**without this `effectiveLoadout()` auto-fills the top three and lesson 1 is already done on arrival** — one of the four bugs that sank the live-hub tour), `SALTIZ_COSMETIC='striker:base'`, `Storage.prototype.setItem` records+drops, `WebSocket.prototype.send` drops `setLoadout`/`setCosmetic`, the 2600ms carousel auto-rotate is filtered out, and the tutorial done-keys are seeded so neither the first-run tutorial nor «מרכז» auto-launches over the lesson.
  - **Completion latches on the BLOCKED writes** (`setSlotCard`→`pikme-loadout`, `setHeroSkinByRarity`→`pikme_cosmetic`), so the lesson needs no socket and no module-private state, and the same record proves nothing escaped.
  - **Three findings from reading the shipped hub, worth keeping:** (1) a card can only be grabbed by **lifting it UP** — `dy < -16` and mostly vertical, else it reads as a carousel swipe (`client.js` ~1596) — and both drop targets sit BELOW the carousel, so every drag is up-then-over, which is what the hand mimes; (2) **`RARITY_SKIN.common === 'base'` and the hero already IS base**, so dropping a COMMON on the hero changes nothing on screen — the rare is the only card with a visible payoff, which is exactly why the user asked for one; (3) **both ends of a drag must be `.tu-live`**: `slotUnder()` uses `elementFromPoint`, which ignores `pointer-events: none`, so a gate that lights only the carousel swallows every drop.
  - **Four faults found by SCREENSHOT after the assertions were already green** (all fixed, all now asserted): the shipped `.veiled` radial spotlight computed to **r=255px** on an 844x390 screen and washed the dim off most of the lobby (dropped — the gate already dims per box, with hard edges); `.hub-tu-gate .hub > *` **cannot reach the stadium backdrop**, which is painted behind `#home` and is not a `.hub` child, so the lesson ran on a fully bright lobby while every per-element check passed (fixed with a scrim inside `.hub` at z-index 1 — above `.hub-pitch`, below every interactive box); the caption moved to the bottom put its **live pip on the «25JUL-v5» build tag** (pips reordered above the text); and the hero's `.lab-drop` dashed box was a **234x246 frame drawn across the power slots** (now a glow, at a specificity that outranks `.tu-live { filter: none }`).
  - **Two traps for whoever works this next:** `.hub-cards .cf-card` is declared TWICE in `style.css` (23x31 at ~1138, then **150x205 at ~2358, which wins**) — the hub's front card is genuinely huge, so reading the first rule mis-diagnoses a correct layout as broken. And `.tu-live { z-index: 3 }` is wrong for `#power-slots` (authored at 6): it would push the slots BEHIND the hero's button box, so the lab lifts lit targets to 7. **z-index only, never `position`** — that is what sent the last tour's ⚽ off-screen.
  - **ROUND 2 (same agent).** User: "make the hand go a bit higher. then show the equipped best." + "make another tutorial about the different element in this screen… darker except the elements ill describe in order and write what each does" with a 13-item list.
    - **Hand higher:** `LIFT` 34 → **48**, and the gesture is now two beats with a hold between them — pull straight UP, hold, then swipe across at that height, then set down. Keyframes: `0.16 (0,-48)` · `0.32 (0,-48)` · `0.56 (102,-48)` · `0.74 (164,-16)`. **The order is the mechanic:** one diagonal sweep is exactly what the carousel classifies as a SWIPE, so the old shape taught a kid to spin the deck.
    - **Lesson 3 «הכי טוב»** (`#select-best-btn`) — one tap fills all three slots. Taught LAST on purpose: first, it would fill every slot before the kid knew what a slot was and lesson 1 would have nothing empty to drop into.
    - **Tour 2, the element legend — `?tour=elements`** (or the «סיור במסך ›» button on the card finale). All 13 in the user's order, each lit while everything else is dark, with a name + one line on what it does. **Nothing navigates: every lit element is left INERT** — a tap on ⚙ or 👥 would open that screen and abandon the tour on step 1. Advance = tap anywhere (catcher at z-index 39, under `#tutorial`/the exit) or «הבא ›».
    - **Lighting ONE element out of a filter gate needs TWO classes.** A parent's filter cannot be undone by a child and the gate dims per `.hub > *`, so lighting `#training-btn` (inside `#play-strip`) = un-dim the strip (`.lab-show`) + dim its other children individually (`.lab-dim`).
    - **THE HAND-OFF BUG, worth knowing for any tour that chains into another:** `finish()` runs INSIDE a tick that has already queued its successor, so starting tour 2 immediately left tour 1's loop alive — two loops on the same globals, double `stepT`, and both able to consume one `done()` and skip a step. Every tick now carries a run token. Symptom was visual, not logical: **the second tour ran on a fully bright lobby while every per-element assertion passed.** Now asserted four ways (gate class, scrim, a dimmed non-target, and 1.0s of wall clock ⇒ ~1.0s of `stepT`).
    - **Two more screenshot-only faults:** the hand (54px) centred on ⚙ **hid the very element being described** — tap steps now anchor the hand just outside the target (below, or above when there is no room), asserted for all 13 as "the hand's box does not contain the target's centre"; and the element tour's second line must never be swapped for the idle nudge, because that line IS the lesson.
  - **ROUND 3 — THE ORDER (user):** "make the home tour first, then the cards pull, first pull a hero custom, then explain each slot, then pull a card to slot, then show the select best". Now **one run of 19 steps**: 1-13 home legend (READ) · 14 rare→hero (DO) · 15-17 what each power slot does (READ) · 18 card→slot (DO) · 19 «הכי טוב» (DO). `?tour=home` / `?tour=cards` run either half alone.
    - **The three slots are explained while still EMPTY** — the only time each shows its own ⚡/🏃/🛡️ glyph instead of a card. Copy is the GAME'S OWN (`SLOT_META` in client.js), shortened; inventing new wording would be a second, drifting description of a buff the server calculates.
    - **The tap-catcher is now PER STEP, not per tour** — one run mixes read and do steps, and a catcher left up over a drag step eats the gesture.
    - **⚠️ A "read-only" step was still TAPPABLE, and fixing it needs `!important`:** the gate's `pointer-events: none` lands on the `.hub > *` box and children inherit it — except **`#power-slots .pslot-item { pointer-events: auto }` (`style.css:2298`)**, which being an ID rule outranks any class chain. A tap on a slot mid-explanation would have opened the cards room and walked the kid off the tour. Read steps now set `body.lab-inert` → `.hub, .hub * { pointer-events: none !important }`. **Found by assertion, not by eye.**
    - **`icon-system.js` REPLACES emoji in the DOM with pixel sprites**, so `textContent` of a caption written as «⚡ בעיטה» comes back as «בעיטה». Assertions on caption text must use `includes`, not equality. (The sprite is wanted — it is the game's own look.)
    - **The caption-clearance assertion was too strict** and failed on a 9px graze: the hero's button box is 246px tall and its bottom edge laps into the caption while the 210px character canvas centred in it is nowhere near. Now: the caption must miss the target's CENTRE and cover ≤25% of its height.

Four screenshots in `_tu-shots/lab-*.png`, all four inspected. `test-tutorial.mjs`: ALL PASS.

Levels only unlock in order, so "an earlier level taught it" is a fact. Output is exactly the user's four buttons — L1/`move`:move · L1/`charge`:aim · L2/`bomb`:bomb · L2/`wall`:wall. Nothing about the hub was reported.

The reason it was suppressed is real — the comic word draws at `H*0.40` with a starburst reaching `S*0.30`, and `.tu-caption` sits at `top:64px`, so on a landscape phone they overlap — so the collision is ANSWERED, not avoided: new `.tutorial.celebrating .tu-caption` (opacity+visibility) steps the coach's words aside for the beat the word is up, and the word is cut to `TU_CELEB_SEC` 0.8s via a new optional `over` argument on `triggerCelebration(kind, over)`. During a goal beat the standing caption is an instruction the kid has just obeyed, so nothing is lost. The class is toggled in `tuTick` ABOVE the freeze return (the whole beat happens inside the freeze) and cleared in `tuExit`/`tuEnter`/`tuHubEnter` — `tuTick` stops running when a level ends on a goal, so without that the hub tour would inherit an invisible caption.

The UN-FREEZE is still `step()`'s own `resetTimer <= 0` branch, and the coach still waits for `resetTimer === 0` before advancing, so the next stage is applied to a body that can move. Applies to every scoring step (L1 `goal`/`super`, L2 `ballshot`) because it is keyed to the goal, not to a step.

New correction **`underHeld`** («החזק יותר — עד שהטבעת מלאה»), the mirror of the tap step's over-hold line, sampled at the RELEASE because letting go early is only knowable once you have let go.

It rides the existing `.tu-cap`, and because the text changes on each tap the caption's pop re-runs — one free beat per tap.

  - **What shipped:** level 4 `mercaz` (🏠, `TU_LEVELS` index 3). Six steps — 🏆 גביעים (dwell) · 🃏 קלפים · ⚡ כוחות (drag) · 🦸 גיבור · 👥 חברים on a **mock lobby the tutorial draws itself** (`tuMockBuild`), then ⚽ on the **real hub**, whose tap starts an actual match. Auto-launches on the first hub visit, skippable (`fbTuHubSkipped`), replayable from 🎓 forever.
  - **`where: 'hub'` on a level = no room, no socket, no stages. `server.js` IS NOT TOUCHED.** `stageAt`/`foeKeys` now tolerate a stageless level; the server's tutorial hooks already guarded `if (!stage) return`, so a client asking for level 4 gets an empty pitch, not a crash.
  - **`tuUnlocked` exemption:** a hub level gates on level 1 ALONE, not the sequential chain — otherwise auto-launch-on-first-hub-visit could never fire.
  - **WHY THE MOCK (please don't re-litigate):** it first ran on the LIVE hub and a browser found four bugs sharing one root — the wardrobe is an OVERLAY not a screen swap; the carousel AUTO-ROTATES and completed a step with zero input; `effectiveLoadout()` auto-fills the top 3 so the drag step was done on arrival; and **localhost injects a sample album** (`DEV_LOCAL`), which hides the empty-album path. **Test on the LAN IP, never localhost.**
  - **⚠️ THREE TRAPS FOR ANY FUTURE HUB-OVERLAY WORK:**
    1. **`#tutorial` lives INSIDE `#game`**, which is `display:none` on the hub — hand/caption/pips render into a hidden subtree while `textContent` assertions pass happily. It is re-homed onto `<body>` for the level and restored on exit.
    2. **Never set `position` on a hub box.** The BAKED LOBBY LAYOUT positions them absolutely; a gate rule with `position: relative` threw the finale's ⚽ to x=1457 in an 844px viewport, off-screen. Use z-index alone.
    3. **Touch pointers keep IMPLICIT CAPTURE** — a drag onto a slot is delivered to the CARD, so the slot never hears the drop. Hit-test the release with `elementFromPoint` (what `bindSlotDrag` always did).
  - **The tutorial writes NOTHING:** guards on `setSlotCard`/`swapSlots`/`saveCosmetic`, because `saveLoadout`/`saveCosmetic` -> `postPrefs()` -> the app persists prefs **under the player's phone number**. Asserted: no `localStorage` write, no app-bridge post.

- **Tutorial onboarding — three scripted pitch levels for kids (agent `tutorial-coach`). SHIPPED + PUSHED.** User: "ok now i want to do a tutorial onboarding for the football game... this is aim for kids so the toturial should be very intiutive and simple. also see what brawlstars and fortnight are doing" -> "do the simplest way and such that can alwys replay the toturial" -> "yes lets build, do this in local host first" -> "now add the second, using bombs building walls, shooting at the ball. detach ball from enemy etc." -> "push for each toturial block" -> "you should also explain that holding the aim the longer make the shoot and kick stronger" -> a gesture-and-wording pass.
  - **Design + rationale: [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-tutorial-onboarding-design.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-tutorial-onboarding-design.md).** Carries the Brawl Stars / Epic research, the level tables, and the alternatives rejected and why.
  - **Commits (all PUSHED):** `a31a46d` level 1 · `4baab4d` level 2 + the charge step + the level picker · `686a72f` level 3 first cut + minDwell + tap-vs-drag · `3c28588` the gesture/wording pass.
  - **What I built:** L1 יסודות (move · tap · hold · kick · super) · L2 קרב (shoot-the-ball · bomb · wall · strip) · L3 טריקים first cut. Silent coach: spotlight + a MIMED hand + 1-2 Hebrew words, never pauses, no clock, no fail state. Level 1 auto-launches once on a first run with no skip; every level is replayable forever from `אימון → 🎓 איך משחקים?`. Celebration-only reward (`c86fa82` "practice pays nothing" holds).
  - **A LEVEL IS DATA.** `shared/tutorial.js` = a `steps` table + a declarative `stages` table that `server.js` interprets (`applyTuStage`/`updateTutorial`/`tutorialPost`). Adding a level is a data change. Keep that module PURE (no DOM/sockets/timers) — that is what makes the rules unit-testable.
  - **`minDwell`** holds a COMPLETED step open N more seconds. Some lessons only land in the seconds after the act (the wall standing there taking fire). `isStepDone` = achieved; `advance()` = achieved AND seen.
  - **Two bugs only the BROWSER caught, both invisible to DOM assertions:** (1) `.tu-hand` positioned itself with a `transform: translate(...)` while its keyframes animate `scale` — CSS applies scale BEFORE transform, so the position vector got scaled and the hand rendered ~110px off a right-hand button, worse while nudging. Position now lives in left/top. (2) A `noClock` tutorial still drew the 2:00 match timer counting down, plus the kickoff countdown banner over the next lesson; `#hud` and `#banner` are hidden for the duration. **Keep `handOnTarget()` in `_tu-verify.mjs`** if you touch the coach layer — it measures the hand's REAL rect against its target's instead of trusting the CSS var, which is the only reason bug (1) was found.

No puppeteer; raw CDP over `ws`.

Nothing of theirs was lost and no code of theirs was touched. Mentioning it because it is exactly the failure CLAUDE.md warns about: **do not rewrite a span of this file you did not write.**

On a screen where every other step's hand moves, a still hand reads as "nothing here". Added `@keyframes tu-tap`: straight down/up twice then a beat, **never sideways** (sideways is what `tu-pull` means — this button gets pressed where it is, not dragged).
    2. **`sub` and `nudge` looked identical.** The new always-on `sub` line ("הקשה = מתחתיך · גרירה = לאן שתכוון") shares `#tu-nudge` with the stuck-nudge, so an escalation was invisible. Now `.tu-caption small` is the calm dark pill and `.tutorial.nudging .tu-caption small` turns amber + breathes, matching the hand that grows/speeds up at the same moment — one signal in two places.
    3. Also gave that pill `max-width: min(86vw, 560px)` + `text-wrap: balance`: the old nudges were four words, a `sub` runs to three clauses, and unconstrained it walks off both screen edges in phone landscape.

- **LEVEL 3 REWORKED: step 1 is now FIND-the-hidden-bot, step 2 flies off a pre-built steel wall (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User: "in the stage 3 of toturial, make the first step the bot hiding and you should find the bot, when you see it it says hide in bush or somthing and go to the next step. in the second step put a steal wall behind the player and tell him to just tap the bomb and aim in the direction he wants to fly."
  - **Files taken: `shared/tutorial.js`, `public/client.js`, `test-tutorial.mjs`.** No `server.js` change was needed — `fieldFor(l)` and the declarative stage table already carry all of it. Committed on top of `3c28588`, so none of the gesture-pass work is in my commit.
  - **Step 1 `hide` → `find`.** The watcher is now planted INSIDE the bush (`TU3_FIND.foe` = the bush centre) and the kid starts ~610px away, so it is genuinely invisible (`BUSH_REVEAL_DIST` 110). Caption «איפה הוא?» → on the sighting it swaps to «הוא היה בשיח!» + «גם אתה יכול להתחבא שם». **The lesson is taught from the other side of the bush**: a kid who has just failed to see somebody works out for themselves that the same bush hides them; being told to go stand in the green teaches only that the coach knows a rule. No `marker` — a cue pointing at the bush would answer the question. `minDwell: 2.5` so both lines get read.
  - **New completion flag `foundFoe`** (replaces `hidden`, which no step used any more). Latched client-side off **`bushRevealAlpha(foe) >= 0.6`** — the renderer's own stealth value, so what completes the step is literally what the kid can see, not a distance that might disagree with the sprite. Needs the snapshot player object, so `tuFoePos` was split into `tuFoeSnap` (object) + `tuFoePos` (x/y wrapper).
  - **New `sub2` + `subFor(l,n,ctx)`** in `shared/tutorial.js`: the second line now swaps on the step's `when` flag exactly like `cap2` does. That swap IS the find step's teaching device. Client's second-line priority is now nudge → `sub2` → `sub`.
  - **Step 2 `fly`: the steel wall is now part of the pitch** (`TU3_WALL`, in `TU3_FIELD.hardWalls`) at `cx 540`, 84px behind the kid's spawn — inside `BOMB_WALL_DIST` (150) so it cannons (×1.24) but not close enough to stand in. **🧱 is no longer in that step's controls at all**: building is level 2's lesson and charging it as rent on level 3's turned one trick into a two-part errand. Caption «תעוף!» / sub «כוון לאן שתרצה לעוף · הקש 💣 · ואל תזוז».
  - **Why "aim then tap" is exact, not a simplification:** a tap-planted bomb sits under your feet, and `explode()` has no radial direction to throw you in when you are dead-centre (`rd > 6` fails), so **it uses your aim**. `applyTuStage` already points every stage's aim at +x, so a kid who taps 💣 and touches nothing else flies right into the open pitch first try. The one failure mode is aiming INTO the steel — `jumpBlocked` cancels the launch outright — so that step's nudge is «כוון לצד הפתוח — לא לקיר», about direction rather than about the button.
  - **`TU3_FIELD`/`TU3_BUSH`/`TU3_WALL` moved DOWN** into the level-3 geometry section, below `MID`. `TU3_WALL` reads `MID` inside an object initializer and the old position was above `MID`'s declaration — that is the exact TDZ shape that killed the whole of `client.js` once already (see the lesson higher up this log).
