## Brawl-Stars drag-distance + cancel + sensitivity for WALL & BOMB (single writer)
- **What**: unified drag-to-aim on both the 🧱 wall-build and 💣 bomb buttons — pull farther = build/throw farther (capped), drag back toward centre = CANCEL with haptic + red-✕ graphics, plus an adjustable sensitivity/reach setting. Researched via 5 subagents (incl. Brawl-Stars web research).
- **Distance**: `aimFrac(len)` (shared) maps drag→0..1 past the deadzone, scaled by sensitivity `aimSensPx`. Bomb reach = `aimFrac*bombMaxPx` (server still hard-caps at BOMB_LOB_RANGE=250). Wall reach = `offset + aimFrac*(wallMaxPx-offset)`, enabled by new `BUILD_DIST_MAX=120` ceiling.
- **Cancel**: shared `updateDragCancel(drag)` state machine w/ hysteresis (CANCEL_ARM_PX=34 out / CANCEL_IN_PX=18 back-in), edge-triggered `haptic('cancel')` (guarded by `wasCancel`), `haptic('rearm')` tick when pulled back out. Release keys off `cancelArmed`. Added VIBE `cancel:[15,40,15]`, `rearm:10`.
- **Graphics**: wall ghost → red + ✕ when cancel-armed (charge ring reddens); bomb ghost → dashed amber trajectory + red landing circle normally, hollow red ring+✕ at feet when cancel-armed. HUD `.cancel-armed` red outline + ✕ on the button (both buttons, CSS).
- **Sensitivity/reach UI**: 3 sliders in the controls-editor overlay (`s-aimSens` 40–160, `s-bombMax` 80–250, `s-wallMax` 60–180). Client-only, persisted to localStorage (`fbAimSens/fbBombMax/fbWallMax`) — NOT in SETTING_KEYS (server never reads them; they apply in real matches). `ce-reset` restores defaults.
- **Defaults preserve today's feel exactly**: wallMax=92 (=offset+thick), bombMax=250. Smoke-verified: wall full-drag→92, raised→180 ceiling; bomb full-drag→250, reach-slider→120; sensitivity shortens finger travel. TDZ-safe (`let` + call-time refs only).
- **Files**: shared/constants.js (+BUILD_DIST_MAX), shared/sim.js (thick→BUILD_DIST_MAX), public/client.js (helpers+handlers+ghosts+sliders), public/index.html (sliders), public/style.css (cancel-armed + ce-sliders). node --check clean; restarted :3012.

- **Tutorial coach layout on a PHONE: heading was too big and too low (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User, item 1 of five: "the screen elements dont align propelly on the phone, the heading is too big and too low."
  - **Both numbers were reasoned about on a desktop window**, where a viewport is wide AND tall. A landscape phone is **844×390**.
    - `top: max(64px, safe-area + 58px)` was holding a hole open for the score/clock cluster — which **does not exist in a tutorial**: `enterMatch` puts `tu-off` on `#hud` (client.js ~4391) precisely so a first-timer isn't watching a countdown to nothing. 64px of a 390px-tall screen is one sixth of it, held empty. Now `max(10px, safe-area + 8px)`.
    - the cap line was sized in **`vw`** — on a landscape phone vw is the LONG axis, so `7.5vw` of 844 = 63px and the only thing limiting it was the 58px ceiling. Now `clamp(20px, min(8vh, 7vw), 42px)`: **vh-first, with the vw term kept as a second ceiling** so a short wide window can't blow it up either (`min()` takes the tighter axis).
  - Same treatment for the second line (`3.2vw` = 27px on a phone, i.e. only the 19px ceiling was holding it → `min(3.6vh, 3.2vw)`) and the pips (fixed 9px dots → `clamp(6px, 1.9vh, 9px)` + `aspect-ratio`). Text-shadow steps 5px/9px → 3px/5px: they were tuned for a 58px cap line and read as a smudge under a 31px one.
  - **Resolved at 844×390:** cap **31.2px** (was 58) · sub **14.0px** (was 19) · pip **7.4px** (was 9) · cluster top **10px** (was 64). Whole stack ~**76px of 390 = 19%** of the screen, against ~39% before. (Arithmetic, not a screenshot.)
  - **"Don't align properly" is only PARTLY addressed.** The three parts are centred in one flex column and I found nothing structurally misaligned by reading — the size/position was the visible problem. If something is still off, it needs a screenshot to diagnose; the no-testing rule this session means nobody has taken one.
  - **Not tested.** `node --check` clean. CSS only, plus this entry.

- **The HUB TOUR (level 4 «מרכז») is PARKED — the tutorial is the three training levels again (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User: "remove the continue to center (the lobby toturial) and leave just the training toturial there."
  - **Switched OFF, not deleted, and deliberately still IN `TU_LEVELS`.** One field on the level — `offered: false` — plus a pure `tuOffered(l)` (`offered !== false`, so offering is opt-OUT and a level added tomorrow is live without anyone remembering this flag). **Delete that one line to put the tour back in the flow.**
  - **Why parked in place rather than filtered out of the array:** the tour is a finished feature (its own design doc, a mock lobby, a real-⚽ finale, ~40 assertions in A16/A17). Left in the array, every helper still addresses it by index and all of that coverage still runs; filtered out, `TU_HUB_LEVEL` becomes -1 and every one of those assertions had to be rewritten or deleted. A product call about what a kid is SHOWN should not cost the ability to change our minds. (I tried the filter first and backed it out for exactly this reason.)
  - **`tuOffered` is the ONE gate for all three ways a level can be reached**, so it cannot end up half-hidden: `nextLevel()` skips it (→ the finale's «המשך ל…» button has nowhere to point, hides itself, and the title becomes «אתה מוכן!»), `renderTuLevels()` skips its row in «איך משחקים?», and `tuMaybeAutoStart`'s hub branch is gated on it (no more auto-launch on a kid's first arrival at the lobby). A parked level is SKIPPED by the `nextLevel` walk, never terminates it — otherwise parking one mid-list would hide every level after it.
  - Verified by pure import: table still 4 levels, offered = `basics,combat,tricks`, `nextLevel` after all three = **null**, hub still addressable at index 3 with its 6 steps.
  - **Tests:** A10's two hub assertions rewritten to the new truth, new **A10b** (offered-ness, opt-out default, parked-but-addressable, walk-skips-parked), A17's title corrected — it claimed the tour auto-launches, which is no longer true even though its unlock rule is unchanged. **Not run** (standing instruction).

- **Play-with-friends room: a by-code joiner could never get IN, and the team page never showed the room number (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User: "you work on room in play with friends, we see the player waiting but not inside the room, i the teams page put the room number."
  - **ROOT CAUSE of "waiting but not inside the room" — traced, not guessed.** A friend joining by CODE does not enter the room: `joinPrivateRoom` (server.js ~1145) puts them in `room.pending`, sends THEM `joinPending` (the «ממתין לאישור המארח…» overlay) and sends the HOST a `joinRequest`. The host's only way to answer was `#room-requests` — **a div inside `#lobby`**. The whole play-with-friends flow lands on **`#party`** instead (`openPartyDirect` → `showScreen('party')`, and every `lobby` payload re-asserts it at client.js ~4283), so `#lobby` is never on screen for the one person holding the decision. The request was **unanswerable**: host sees a friend "waiting", friend waits for ever, nobody is told why. (The INVITE path was never broken — `partyRespond` auto-admits, server.js ~1922 — so this only ever bit the code path.)
  - **Fix:** a second copy of the panel, `#party-requests`, inside `#party` right above the roster, and `renderRoomRequests()` now fills **every** container (`roomRequestHosts()`) instead of one element. `decideRequest` re-renders both, so answering on one screen clears the row from the other and the two copies cannot disagree. No server change — the approval gate is untouched, it is now merely reachable.
  - **Room number on the team page:** new `#party-code-wrap` / `#party-code` chip in `#party`'s head, reusing `.lobby-code-wrap`'s type so it reads identically to the #lobby one, pushed to the far end of the flex head. **Tap copies** (falls back to a toast showing the code — `clipboard.writeText` needs a secure context and the LAN surface is plain http, so that path is real).
  - Two traps handled: `renderPartyCode` runs **before** `renderParty`'s no-rebuild signature guard (the code arrives on `roomJoined` while the roster is byte-identical, so a chip updated after the guard would sit at «····» until somebody joined); and `openPartyDirect` now clears `roomCode`, or a fresh party rendered before `roomJoined` flashes the PREVIOUS room's number — a number a kid might read out to a friend.
  - `.party-code-wrap` uses `font-family: inherit`, NOT `font: inherit` — the shorthand also reset the size/weight/letter-spacing `.lobby-code-wrap` supplies on the same element and the chip came out at body size.
  - **Verified:** `node --check` clean; `test-party`, `test-party-invite-flow`, `test-party-3v3-chat`, `test-back-nav` all pass. **NOT seen in a browser** — the two-client dance (host on the team page + a second client joining by code) needs a live harness nobody has written; the host-approval round trip is the thing to eyeball.

- **FAIRNESS CAP shipped: every device now sees the same pitch, and Codex's sky fills a tablet's surplus (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User: "ive noticed in ipad the player sees more of the game which is unfair, how can we make it fair?" → then, after the artifact and the sky-band prompt → "see what codex did http://127.0.0.1:3012/_sky-band.html add it and simulate in ipad and iphone".
  - **THE BUG, measured:** `scale = CAM_ZOOM * wbW / FIELD.W` derives zoom from WIDTH alone, so every device saw the same 1212 world units across and however many units of HEIGHT its screen was worth: phone **560**, iPad mini 796, iPad Pro 11" 847, **iPad Pro 13" 909** — up to **+62% more pitch** for the tablet player. Same width, free vertical vision.
  - **The fix (Option C of `docs`/the fairness artifact): a fixed play window + mask, NOT a zoom change.** `PLAY_H = 540` world units; `playH = min(wbH, PLAY_H*scale)`; `bandY = (wbH - playH)/2`. **`scale` is untouched** — the width fit was already binding on every real device, so sprite sizes, the baked background and every existing draw call are unchanged. `updateCamera`'s vertical clamp now works against `playH` instead of `wbH` (that substitution IS the fix), `wy()` gained a `viewOffY` so the window sits below the band with no other call-site changes, and `renderBackground` zeroes that offset while it bakes.
  - **Why 540 and not the phone's 560:** the cap can only take view AWAY, so a phone SHORTER than the reference cannot reach 560 — a 360dp Galaxy A15 tops out at 546, which left the shortest phones at the disadvantage. 540 is under every supported phone's ceiling so all of them land on it exactly. Costs the reference iPhone 20 units (3.6%).
  - **What fills the band — the rule the user set:** sky ONLY where the band covers PITCH; where the window edge has crossed a touchline and the band overlaps the REAL terrace, the stands show through and scroll with the world as they always did. `drawSkyBands()` computes that from the pitch's top/bottom edge in ART px, so the handover is geometric, not a guess. Filling the whole band with terrace art was tried first and rejected by the user on sight — the band is screen-fixed, so world-locked stands slide inside a static frame and read as broken.
  - **A 1-2 ART px band is left in place deliberately** (rounding on a 360dp Android). Snapping it shut and handing the screen back was tried and is WORSE: that device then sees 546 against everyone else's 540, i.e. the exact inequality being removed. The band stays; the sliver is painted flat in the sky's base tone instead of composing scenery into it.
  - **Codex's art wired in as-is:** `public/sky-band.js` (`window.SkyBand.draw(ctx, rect, {camX, t, side, edgeApproach, bannerText})`, its own lab at `public/_sky-band.html`), added to `index.html` before `hub-tour.js`. Its API took `edgeApproach` (0 midfield → 1 at the edge) rather than the `worldTop/worldBottom` the prompt asked for; the caller computes it, so no change was needed on their side.
  - **VERIFIED, not asserted.** New `_fair-check.mjs` reads a new `window.__view()` hook (same spirit as `HubTour.state()`) in a real match on five surfaces: **every device sees 1212×540, spread 0 units**, nobody exceeds the window, tablets have a band (93/128/71 ART px), phones at most a 4px sliver. Screenshots in `_device-shots/fair-*.png`. `_device-matrix.mjs` still clean on all five.
  - **TWO THINGS I DID NOT FIX (not mine, both visible in the screenshots):** (1) the **hub tour's overlay bleeds over a live match** — its mock-lobby cards and pointing hand paint over the left of the pitch even with `fbHubTourDone`+`fbHubTourSkipped` set and `.screen` = `game`; (2) ~~the blimp banner's Hebrew renders reversed~~ — **WRONG, retracted.** `sky-band.js` hand-builds a 27-glyph Hebrew pixel font and draws it RTL correctly ("logical first character at the right edge, then the pen moves left"); what I read as garbled was its default string «שחקו יחד» at 2px-per-pixel. Nobody should chase this. The hub-tour leak in (1) is real.

- **Sky band: drift made visible, parallax depth un-inverted, banner says «סולטיז» (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User: "i dont see the claouds animation as expected" → then "the sky paralex is built inverted, look again in /_sky-band.html · also put in the air ballon סולטיז".
  - **The drift was live but imperceptible, and that was MY brief's fault.** The prompt asked for "clouds drift a few units per second" and never converted ART px into what an eye sees: at `ART_PX 3.25`, 0.65 art px/s is 2.1 device px/s = **21.6 minutes to cross an iPad**, against a match lasting minutes. One `MOTION = 5` factor now scales every rate, preserving Codex's depth layering: near cloud crosses in 99s, far in 259s, blimp in 40s. Verified by pixel-diffing clipped captures of the band on a live client (`_sky-motion.mjs`), not by reading constants.
  - **The parallax inversion was REAL, and it was a DEPTH inversion, not a direction one.** Measured on the lab page with `_sky-parallax.mjs` (tracks the red balloon and blue blimp centroids across a 2000px camera pan, drift paused): direction was always correct — sky moves LEFT as camX rises, same as the world. What was backwards is that the module's own "distant outer sky" objects carried the LARGEST coefficients (balloons 0.026, blimp 0.045) while the cloud bank hugging the touchline had the smallest (0.008–0.018), so the furthest things slid fastest. **Now monotonic with distance: near clouds 0.018 > far clouds 0.008 > balloons 0.005 > blimp 0.003.** Blimp shift across the same pan went 89px → 6px. **Self-drift deliberately untouched** — a blimp under power may cross faster than the clouds behind it, which reads as an aircraft, not as depth.
  - **Banner: «סולטיז»**, passed from `client.js` (`SKY_BANNER`) so the module stays generic scenery and the brand lives in the game; the module default was changed to match so the lab shows what ships. All six glyphs were already in Codex's font, so nothing silently dropped.
  - **Harnesses left behind:** `_sky-motion.mjs` (is it moving?) and `_sky-parallax.mjs` (which way, and does depth fall with distance?). Both answer questions a screenshot cannot.

- **FIXED: `edgeApproach` was handed to SkyBand INVERTED, which flattened the whole sky→stadium transition (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User: "the sky only appear where the extra area is to cover the field! when the user approaches the field the sky paralaxes so the stadium appears first and the sky just go away… like the stadium appears slowly down from the sky".
  - **The bug was mine, in `drawSkyBands`, not in Codex's art.** The module's contract is `edgeApproach` = **0 at midfield, 1 at the stadium edge**. I computed it from the pitch edge's DISTANCE — and at midfield that distance is NEGATIVE (the pitch edge sits above the band), so it clamped to **1.00 at midfield and 0.00 at the touchline**. Approach fired hardest in the middle of the pitch and switched off at the line, so the cloud-push cue the art was built around never ran when it mattered.
  - **Now measured as the stadium's INTRUSION into the band** (`intrusionArt / bandY`), which is the same quantity the sky is being clipped by — so the module's cloud-push and the sky's shrinking edge move as one piece and the stands read as descending out of the sky.
  - **Verified end to end, not by reading constants:** new `_sky-approach.mjs` drives the REAL move stick upward on a 1366×1024 iPad in a live match and samples the transition every 0.6s. Result: `0/128 → 48/128 → 109/128 → 128/128` art px of stadium in the top band as the player walks to the touchline — progressive, monotonic, sky shrinking to nothing. Asserts all three properties (starts sky, ends stadium, arrives gradually).
  - `window.__view()` now also reports `camY`, `stadiumTop`, `stadiumBot` so this transition is observable by any harness rather than taken on trust.

- **Sky composition brought in line with the APPROVED SPEC (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User pasted Codex's required composition and «the skies should be above the field». Two deviations of mine, both fixed:
  1. **`PLAY_H` 540 → 560.** The spec's window is 1212×560, and 560 is exactly what the reference iPhone (844×390 @ CAM_ZOOM 1.65) shows — which is what makes **the iPhone's band come out 0**, i.e. an iPhone draws no sky at all, as the spec requires. My 540 (chosen to buy exact parity with a 360dp Android that tops out at 545) gave the iPhone a 4px band and so broke "on iPhone the gameplay window fills the screen". Residual, stated rather than hidden: a 360dp Android sees 546 of 560, i.e. **2.7% less**, with band 0.
  2. **The band is now ALWAYS SKY — no world content may show through.** I had been letting the real terrace appear in the band wherever the window edge crossed a touchline. That is not this composition and it is wrong twice: it puts world geometry in a screen-fixed frame (the sliding artefact that killed the stadium-in-the-band idea), and it means two players at the same camera position see different things depending on screen height — the exact inequality the cap exists to remove. The stands now arrive in the WINDOW; `edgeApproach` pushes the cloud bank in sympathy.
  - **Verified against the spec's own acceptance criteria** (`_fair-check.mjs`, `_sky-approach.mjs`): window **1212×560** on every device that can fill it (±1 unit — `wbW` is a `ceil`, so the iPhone lands at 559, 0.18%); **phones draw NO sky (band 0)**; tablets banded 64/87/121 art px; the stadium ramps 0→100% into view as a player walks to the touchline; and the band measures **0% turf-coloured / 13.2% cloud-white** at the touchline — pure composed sky, no pitch, no stands.
  - **NEW CAPABILITY worth knowing about: `_png.mjs`.** The game canvas is **tainted by cross-origin card art**, so `getImageData()` throws `SecurityError` and NO in-page pixel check can ever work — that is why three of my earlier probes silently returned `undefined`. `_png.mjs` decodes Chrome's clipped screenshots (8-bit, non-interlaced, RGB/RGBA) so pixel claims can be measured. Any agent making a visual claim about this game should use it.

- **SKIP button for the football tutorial + proof that iPhone and iPad see the same field (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User: "1. make a skip button for the football toturial for the actual game." · "2. make sure the ipad and the iphone both see the same field. only the ipad should ahve the skyes were it has bigger proportions then the iphone".
  - **(1) «דלג ›» in the coach layer** (`#tu-skip` in index.html, `.tu-skip` in style.css, handler in client.js). The tutorial was unskippable BY DESIGN (Brawl-Stars reasoning: a first-timer has nothing better to do); that is wrong for a reinstall, a parent handing the phone over, or anyone who already knows how to play. Placed top-inline-end — the one corner the coach never uses (caption owns top-centre, sticks own both bottom corners) — measured **266px from the move stick** and given a 65×34 touch target so a child reaching for the stick cannot hit it. `pointer-events: auto` because `.tutorial` is `pointer-events: none`.
  - **Skipping is recorded SEPARATELY from finishing** (`fbTutorialSkipped`, mirroring the hub tour's own key). Marking a skipped level "done" would be a lie the rest of the feature acts on — the picker would star it, `nextLevel` would offer the one after it, and an accidental tap could never be undone. The flag suppresses only the AUTO-LAUNCH; «איך משחקים?» still lists and unlocks every level exactly as before. So it means "not now", never "never".
  - **Verified end to end** (`_tu-skip.mjs`, new): a first run auto-starts → the button is on screen at 65×34 → tapping it lands on the hub → the skip is recorded → nothing is marked done → a reload no longer forces the tutorial → «איך משחקים?» still lists all three levels.
  - **⚠️ A CSS BUG I INTRODUCED AND CAUGHT, worth reading if you edit style.css by script:** inserting before the first match of `.tu-caption {` landed my block INSIDE the `.tutorial.celebrating .tu-caption` rule — splitting it, so my styles only applied during a celebration AND leaving a global `.tu-caption { opacity: 0; visibility: hidden }` that would have hidden the coach's words in every tutorial. The button rendered 42×21px and swallowed no taps, which is how the test found it. Repaired; the celebrating rule is whole again. **Match on the full selector, not its tail.**
  - **(2) Same field on both, proven by PIXELS** (`_same-field.mjs`, new): both devices are put at the tutorial's deterministic level-1 spawn, each one's PLAY WINDOW is captured and decoded, and the two are reduced to a 16×8 grid of mean colours. Result: **iPhone 1212×559 / band 0**, **iPad 1212×560 / band 87 art px**, and the two windows' pictures differ by **4.7/255 mean per cell** — the same field, drawn at different sprite scales. Sky exists only in the iPad's extra proportion, exactly as asked.
  - **NOT mine, still broken:** `_tu-verify.mjs` now reports 26 failures, all because the HUB TOUR hijacks the first run (its first caption reads «הגדרות» where the pitch tutorial's should read «זוז!»). That is the hub-tour intercept/leak already logged above, not the skip button — a workflow agent is on it.

- **ROOT CAUSE FOUND for "the iPad and the iPhone see different things": `viewOffX`/`viewOffY` were declared and NEVER ASSIGNED (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User, with a side-by-side of both devices in the SAME training-ground match with a stationary player: "they both see diffrent things!!!"
  - **The bug:** the band mask sat at `bandY` but the world was still drawn from screen y = 0, because the offsets that were supposed to slide the world into the window were never given a value. So a tablet's window showed the world region **shifted DOWN by its band depth** — ~143 world units on an iPad Pro 11 — while a phone with no band showed it unshifted. Same camera, different slice of pitch. `camX/camY` are documented as "world at the window's top-left corner" and were not, which is exactly the class of bug a comment cannot catch.
  - **Why my earlier "same field" check missed it:** `_same-field.mjs` compares a 16×8 grid of MEAN COLOURS, and a 143-unit vertical shift across near-uniform turf barely moves a mean. It reported 4.7/255 and passed. The lesson: to prove two views match, compare a WORLD RECT or a distinctive feature's position, not average colour. New `_win-rect.mjs` reads `__view().winWorld` — the world rectangle actually inside the window — which is the metric that cannot lie.
  - **AND the geometry is now symmetric, per the user's spec** ("fit the sides for all devices then calculate the height ratio, and if its bigger then put the skies. if the device is extra long add more stadium to the sides"): the play window is a fixed **1212×560 world rect** and `scale = min(wbW/PLAY_W, wbH/PLAY_H)` FITS it. Whichever axis binds, the other's surplus becomes band — **sky above/below for squarer screens, STADIUM left/right for longer ones**. `PLAY_W` is derived as `FIELD.W / CAM_ZOOM`, so CAM_ZOOM stays the one knob. The horizontal camera clamp now uses `playW` (same substitution the vertical one already had).
  - **This also fixes a case nobody had noticed:** before, a LONGER screen silently saw LESS pitch (height = 1212/aspect). A phone whose WebView loses 60px to an inset was down to 475 units against the reference 560 — a 15% disadvantage. It now gets 41 art px of side stadium and the full 560.
  - **Verified across 7 real viewports** (`_win-rect.mjs`): window **1212.1×560 at world origin (−251, 270) on every single one** — same size AND same origin, which is the part that was broken; tablets get sky and no side bands; phones get no sky; the inset phone gets side stadium. `_fair-check.mjs` and `_sky-approach.mjs` still green (`seesWorldW` had to change from `wbW/scale` to `playW/scale` — the old one counted masked side band as vision and read 1244 on a Galaxy A15).
  - **CONFIRMED ON REAL DEVICES, in the app, with the on-screen readout:** iPhone 17 Pro (WebView 874×402, dpr 2 raw 3, scale 0.4429) and iPad Pro 11" (WebView 1210×834, dpr 2, scale 0.6146) both report **sees 1212×560** and **window 1212×560 @ world origin (−251, 116)** — identical size AND origin. Bands: phone `1,0` art px (rounding → side stadium), iPad `0,85` (sky top and bottom). Screenshots show the same slice of pitch on both. The app needed one line to pass `?viewdebug=1` in dev (`pikmeTV-saltiz` commit) since the football screen rebuilds the WebView URL every launch.

- **Applied `uiiphoneskills/SKILL.md` (pixel-canvas-scaling) — the blit factor was FRACTIONAL and differed per device (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User: "use the skills in /Users/adamleeperelman/Documents/pikeme/uiiphoneskills and try to fix this again! make sure to not change the actual fileds in the game!!!"
  - **Invariant 1 was violated and it is a large part of why the two devices "looked different" even after the window was provably identical.** `ART_PX` was **3.25**, so the buffer→canvas blit came out at **3.2491× on an iPhone 17 Pro and 3.2483× on an iPad Pro 11** — fractional, and different per device. An art pixel therefore covered 3 hardware pixels here and 4 there, in a different pattern on each screen: the grid wobbles and the two shimmer differently. Matches the skill's diagnosis row "tiles unevenly sized, grid wobbles → fractional scale factor".
  - **`ART_PX` is now 3 (integer)**, the buffer is FLOORED (`floor(canvas.width / ART_PX)` — ceil would make the buffer bigger than the canvas and reintroduce a fractional blit), and the blit is exactly `wbW*3 × wbH*3` device px centred with `blitX/blitY`, leftover **≤2 device px** per axis and cleared rather than stretched into. Art is ~8% finer than before. **No field, arena or gameplay data was touched.**
  - **Rotation lifecycle hardened** (skill invariant 4 + its rotation section): a bare `resize` listener is not enough on iOS because `orientationchange` fires BEFORE layout settles and a rotation emits a burst of intermediate sizes — each one reallocating and CLEARING the canvas. Now debounced (100ms) → `requestAnimationFrame` → measure, ignoring the zero/absurd boxes iOS reports mid-rotation, plus `orientationchange` and `visualViewport.resize` as additional nudges. `devicePixelRatio` is re-read on every measure, never cached.
  - **`#canvas` height `100vh` → `100dvh`**: on iOS `100vh` is the LARGE viewport (chrome collapsed), so a full-bleed canvas overflows and the bottom of the pitch sits under the home indicator.
  - **Verified:** `_win-rect.mjs` at the REAL device viewports — window **1212.1×560 at world origin (−251, 270) on all 7**, sky bands 69/94/131 art px on tablets, 0 on phones, side stadium 44 art px on an inset phone. `_fair-check.mjs` and `_sky-approach.mjs` green (band 0.07% turf / 12.4% cloud-white). Blit factor now exactly **3** on every device with ≤2px letterbox.
  - **NOT done from the skill, deliberately:** it recommends the RN host pass the box down (`useWindowDimensions` + insets) instead of the web layer measuring the viewport. Our WebView is full-bleed so `innerWidth/innerHeight` already IS the box; that change is app-side and worth doing only if the football screen ever gains chrome.
  - **Observation, not a bug:** on the hub the overlay reads `UNSIZED (pre-match)` — `resize()` runs at match entry (client.js ~4531), so the canvas has no size until a match starts. Confirmed by reading the call site, not assumed.
